## The Preface1 / 序言

===Original===
This Comedy has been received with universal acceptance, for it was in every part excellently performed; and there needs no other applause of the actors but that they excelled according to the dignity and difficulty of the character they represented. But this great favour done to the work in acting renders the expectation still the greater from the author, to keep up the spirit in the representation of the closet, or any other circumstance of the reader, whether alone or in company; to which I can only say that it must be remembered, a play is to be seen, and is made to be represented with the advantage of action, nor can appear but with half the spirit without it. For the greatest effect of a play in reading is to excite the reader to go to see it; and when he does so, it is then a play has the effect of example and precept.
The chief design of this was to be an innocent performance, and the audience have abundantly shown how ready they are to support what is visibly intended that way. Nor do I make any difficulty to acknowledge that the whole was writ for the sake of the scene of the Fourth Act, wherein Mr. Bevil evades the quarrel with his friend, and2 hope it may have some effect upon the Goths and Vandals that frequent the theatres, or a more polite audience may supply their absence.
But this incident, and the case of the father and daughter, are esteemed by some people no subjects of comedy; but I cannot be of their mind, for anything that has its foundation in happiness and success must be allowed to be the object of comedy; and sure it must be an improvement of it to introduce a joy too exquisite for laughter, that can have no spring but in delight, which is the case of this young lady. I must, therefore, contend that the tears which were shed on that occasion flowed from reason and good sense, and that men ought not to be laughed at for weeping till we are come to a more clear notion of what is to be imputed to the hardness of the head and the softness of the heart; and I think it was very politely said of Mr. Wilks, to one who told him there was a General3 weeping for Indiana, “I’ll warrant he’ll fight ne’er the worse for that.” To be apt to give way to the impressions of humanity, is the excellence of a right disposition and the natural working of a well-turned spirit. But as I have suffered by critics who have got no farther than to inquire whether they ought to be pleased or not,4 I would willingly find them properer matter for their employment, and revive here a song which was omitted for want of a performer, and designed for the entertainment of Indiana. Signor Carbonelli,5 instead of it, played6 on the fiddle, and it is for want of a singer that such advantageous things are said of an instrument which were designed for a voice. The song is the distress of a lovesick maid, and may be a fit entertainment for some small critics7 to examine whether the passion is just or the distress male or female.

===Chinese===
这部喜剧受到了普遍的欢迎，因为各处都演得极为出色；对演员们无须另有赞辞，只消说一句：他们各按所扮人物的身份与难度，皆臻于极致。然而，演出给予这部作品的厚爱，反使人们对作者的期望更增一层，指望他也能在书斋的搬演中——无论读者独处抑或与友共读——保持这股精神；对此我只能说：必须记住，戏剧本是给人观看的，本是要借表演之利搬上舞台，若离了表演，至多只能显出一半的精神。因为阅读中一出戏最大的效用，便是激起读者前去观看；待他果真前去，戏剧这才发挥出典范与训诲的作用。
本剧的主要意图在于成为一出无邪的作品，而观众已充分表明，凡显然以此为目标之作，他们是何等乐于扶助。我亦毫不迟疑地承认，全剧正是为第四幕那一场戏而写——贝维尔先生在那一幕里避开了与友人的争执，2并希望它或能对常出入剧院的哥特人与汪达尔人生出几分效用，再不然，更文雅的观众也自可补其缺位。
然而，这一情节，连同那对父女的境遇，在有些人看来竟算不得喜剧的题材；我却不能苟同，因为凡以幸福与圆满为根基的事物，都须承认可以成为喜剧的对象；何况，引入一种精致得容不下欢笑的喜悦——它只能发源于欢愉，正如这位少女的情形——想必更算得喜剧的一种精进。因此我必得坚持：那一场合所洒的泪，是发自理性与良知的；人们不该因流泪而遭哂笑，除非我们先对何者当归之于头脑之硬、何者当归之于心肠之软，有了更明白的见解。记得有人告诉威尔克斯先生（Mr. Wilks），说有一位将军3正为印第安娜落泪，他的答话极为文雅：“我敢担保，他打起仗来绝不会因此稍逊。”易受人情之所感，正是良好性情的卓越之处，也是一副陶冶得宜的心灵的自然运化。只是我既已受过批评家们的苦——他们的本领止于追问自己该不该喜欢4——我便甘愿为他们寻些更相宜的事做，在此重刊一支歌：它因缺少演唱者而被删去，原是为娱悦印第安娜而设的。卡博内利先生（Signor Carbonelli）5临场代之，奏6了小提琴；只因缺一位歌者，才把原为歌喉而设的种种美誉，都加在了一件乐器身上。这首歌写一位害相思病的少女的愁苦，或正可供某些小小的批评家7消遣，去考究那情感真不真，那愁苦的主角是男是女。

===Original===
> From place to place forlorn I go,
 

 With downcast eyes a silent shade,
 

 Forbidden to declare my woe;
 

 To speak till spoken to, afraid.

> My inward pangs, my secret grief,
 

 My soft consenting looks betray.
 

 He loves, but gives me no relief;
 

 Why speaks not he who may?

===Chinese===
> 我凄惶地一路漂泊，


 眼帘低垂，似无声的暗影，


 被禁止说出我的悲苦；


 不待人先问，便不敢开言。

> 我内心的绞痛，隐秘的忧伤，


 全被温柔默许的眼色泄露。


 他爱着，却不给我解脱；


 有权开口的人，为何不作声？

===Original===
It remains to say a word concerning Terence, and I am extremely surprised to find what Mr. Cibber told me prove a truth:8 that what I valued myself so much upon﻿—the translation of him9﻿—should be imputed to me as a reproach. Mr. Cibber’s zeal for the work, his care and application in instructing the actors and altering the disposition of the scenes, when I was, through sickness, unable to cultivate such things myself, has been a very obliging favour and friendship to me. For this reason I was very hardly persuaded10 to throw away Terence’s celebrated funeral, and take only the bare authority of the young man’s character; and how I have worked it into an Englishman, and made use of the same circumstances of discovering a daughter when we least hoped for one, is humbly submitted to the learned reader.

===Chinese===
临了，还须就泰伦斯（Terence）说上一句。西伯先生（Mr. Cibber）曾对我说过一桩事，如今眼见它应验成真，8我惊讶至极：我最引以自矜的——对他作品的翻译9——竟被归到我头上，成了一桩罪责。西伯先生为本剧的热忱，他在教习演员、改动场次安排上的尽心与勤勉——当其时我因病已无力亲自打理这些——于我是极可感的厚意与情谊。正因如此，我是被万般劝说不过，10才舍弃了泰伦斯那场著名的葬礼，只保留“这位青年品格”这一层孤本的依据；至于我如何把他改造成一个英国人，又如何沿用“在最不敢指望之处认出一位女儿”的同样情节，谨恭呈博学的读者垂鉴。

## Act III28 / 第三幕

===Original===
Scene I. Sealand’s House.
*Enter **Tom**, meeting **Phillis**.*
Tom
Well, Phillis! What, with a face as if you had never seen me before!﻿—What a work have I to do now? She has seen some new visitant at their house whose airs she has caught, and is resolved to practise them upon me. Numberless are the changes she’ll dance through before she’ll answer this plain question: videlicet, have you delivered my master’s letter to your lady? Nay, I know her too well to ask an account of it in an ordinary way; I’ll be in my airs as well as she. *Aside.*﻿—Well, madam, as unhappy as you are at present pleased to make me, I would not, in the general, be any other than what I am. I would not be a bit wiser, a bit richer, a bit taller, a bit shorter than I am at this instant. *Looking steadfastly at her.*
Phillis
Did ever anybody doubt, Master Thomas, but that you were extremely satisfied with your sweet self?
Tom
I am, indeed. The thing I have least reason to be satisfied with is my fortune, and I am glad of my poverty. Perhaps if I were rich I should overlook the finest woman in the world, that wants nothing but riches to be thought so.

===Chinese===
第一场　西兰家。
*汤姆（Tom）上，遇见菲利斯（Phillis）。*
汤姆
哎，菲利斯！怎么，摆出一副好像从没见过我的面孔！——瞧我如今可有得忙了。她准是在府里见了什么新客，学来人家的做派，打定主意要拿我来演练。不等她回答我这句直截了当的问话——即：你把我主人的信交给你们小姐了没有？——她先要变出数不清的花样来。不，我太知道她的为人了，不能用寻常路数向她讨回音；她要摆架子，我也摆我的架子。*旁白。*——好啦，小姐，您眼下尽管乐意叫我受委屈，可总的说来，我仍宁愿做此刻的自己，不愿做别的什么。我不愿比此刻的我聪明一分、阔气一分、高一分、矮一分。*目不转睛地望着她。*
菲利斯
托马斯少爷，谁还不信您对您那可爱的自己满意到了极点呢？
汤姆
我确实满意。最没什么可满意的是我的财运，可我正为自己的穷高兴。倘若我发了财，说不定会看不上天下最美的女人——她如今只差钱财，便人人称她最美了。

===Original===
Phillis
How prettily was that said! But I’ll have a great deal more before I’ll say one word. *Aside.*
Tom
I should, perhaps, have been stupidly above her had I not been her equal; and by not being her equal, never had opportunity of being her slave. I am my master’s servant for hire﻿—I am my mistress’s from choice, would she but approve my passion.
Phillis
I think it’s the first time I ever heard you speak of it with any sense of the anguish, if you really do suffer any.
Tom
Ah, Phillis! can you doubt, after what you have seen?

===Chinese===
菲利斯
这话说得多漂亮！可我还得多磨一阵，才肯吐一个字。*旁白。*
汤姆
倘若我不是她的同等身份，恐怕会蠢得高攀不起她；而正因不是她的同等身份，才有了做她奴仆的机会。我给主人当差是为受雇——我给这位小姐当差是出于拣选，只要她肯见怜我这份情意。
菲利斯
我记得这还是头一遭，听你说起这事时真带着几分痛苦的滋味——倘若你果真痛苦的话。
汤姆
哎，菲利斯！见识过那一切之后，你还能疑心？

===Original===
Phillis
I know not what I have seen, nor what I have heard; but since I am at leisure, you may tell me when you fell in love with me; how you fell in love with me; and what you have suffered or are ready to suffer for me.
Tom
Oh, the unmerciful jade! when I am in haste about my master’s letter. But I must go through it. *Aside.*﻿—Ah!29 too well I remember when, and how, and on what occasion I was first surprised. It was on the 1st of April, 1715, I came into Mr. Sealand’s service; I was then a hobbledehoy, and you a pretty little tight girl, a favourite handmaid of the housekeeper. At that time we neither of us knew what was in us. I remember I was ordered to get out of the window, one pair of stairs, to rub the sashes clean; the person employed on the inner side was your charming self, whom I had never seen before.
Phillis
I think I remember the silly accident. What made ye, you oaf, ready to fall down into the street?
Tom
You know not, I warrant you﻿—you could not guess what surprised me. You took no delight when you immediately grew wanton in your conquest, and put your lips close, and breathed upon the glass, and when my lips approached, a dirty cloth you rubbed against my face, and hid your beauteous form! When I again drew near, you spit, and rubbed, and smiled at my undoing.

===Chinese===
菲利斯
我不知道我见了什么，也不知我听了什么；不过既然我眼下清闲，你可以说说：你几时爱上我的；怎么爱上我的；为我受过什么苦，又预备受什么苦。
汤姆
噢，好个狠心的人儿！偏偏赶上我急着送主人的信。可这一关总得闯。*旁白。*——啊！29 我记得太清楚了：什么时候、怎样、因着什么机缘，我头一回被击中。那是 1715 年 4 月 1 日，我进西兰先生（Mr. Sealand）家当差；那时我是个半大不小的愣小子，你是个秀气利落的小姑娘，是管家婆跟前得宠的丫头。那时节我们俩谁也不知道自己心里装着什么。记得有一回，上头吩咐我爬出二楼的窗子去擦窗棂；在里面擦拭的正是你那迷人的化身，我从前可从没见过你。
菲利斯
那桩傻事我倒还记得。你这个笨货，怎么弄得险些跌到街上去？
汤姆
我担保你并不知道——你猜不着当时叫我吃惊的是什么。你一发觉胜券在握，立刻撒起野来，把嘴唇凑近，往玻璃上呵气；等我的嘴唇凑过去，你却把一块脏布往我脸上一抹，把你那姣好的身影遮住了！等我再挨近，你又啐、又擦，望着我狼狈直笑。

===Original===
Phillis
What silly thoughts you men have!
Tom
We were Pyramus and Thisbe﻿—but ten times harder was my fate. Pyramus could peep only through a wall; I saw her, saw my Thisbe in all her beauty, but as much kept from her as if a hundred walls between﻿—for there was more: there was her will against me. Would she but yet relent! O Phillis! Phillis! shorten my torment, and declare you pity me.
Phillis
I believe it’s very sufferable; the pain is not so exquisite but that you may bear it a little longer.
Tom
Oh! my charming Phillis, if all depended on my fair one’s will, I could with glory suffer﻿—but, dearest creature, consider our miserable state.

===Chinese===
菲利斯
你们男人的念头多傻！
汤姆
我们就是皮拉摩斯与提斯柏（Pyramus and Thisbe）——可我的命运还苦上十倍。皮拉摩斯至多隔墙窥望；我看得见她，看得见我的提斯柏千娇百媚，却与她隔绝得如同中间砌了百堵墙——因为更有甚者：她一颗心不肯依我。她如今肯回心转意才好！噢，菲利斯！菲利斯！缩短我的煎熬吧，说一声你怜惜我。
菲利斯
我想这点苦还受得住；这痛也不算酷烈，你不妨再捱些时日。
汤姆
噢！我迷人的菲利斯，倘若一切全凭我心上人的心意，我受苦也受得荣耀——可是最亲的人儿，想想咱们可怜的处境吧。

===Original===
Phillis
How! Miserable!
Tom
We are miserable to be in love, and under the command of others than those we love; with that generous passion in the heart, to be sent to and fro on errands, called, checked, and rated for the meanest trifles. Oh, Phillis! you don’t know how many china cups and glasses my passion for you has made me break. You have broke my fortune as well as my heart.
Phillis
Well, Mr. Thomas, I cannot but own to you that I believe your master writes and you speak the best of any men in the world. Never was woman so well pleased with a letter as my young lady was with his; and this is an answer to it. *Gives him a letter.*
Tom
This was well done, my dearest; consider, we must strike out some pretty livelihood for ourselves by closing their affairs. It will be nothing for them to give us a little being of our own, some small tenement, out of their large possessions. Whatever they give us, it will be more than what they keep for themselves. One acre with Phillis would be worth a whole county without her.

===Chinese===
菲利斯
怎么！可怜？
汤姆
咱们相爱，却须听命于所爱的人以外的人，这才可怜；心里揣着那样高贵的热情，却被差来遣去跑腿，为顶顶不值钱的鸡毛蒜皮挨呼喝、受申斥。噢，菲利斯！你不知道我这份情意害我打碎了多少瓷杯和玻璃盏。你把我的心打碎了，也把我的前程打碎了。
菲利斯
好吧，托马斯先生，我不能不向你承认：你主人写的信、你说话的口才，天下男人里头要数第一。从来没有什么女人像我们小姐读他那封信那样欢喜；这便是给他的回信。*递给他一封信。*
汤姆
这事办得好，我最亲的；想想看，他们的事一了结，咱们也须给自己谋一条像样的生计。以他们那份大家业，拨出一点小小的产业、一所小小的宅子给咱们，于他们算不得什么。他们无论给咱们什么，都比他们留给自己的多。有菲利斯在，一亩地也抵得过没有她的整整一郡。

===Original===
Phillis
O, could I but believe you!
Tom
If not the utterance, believe the touch of my lips. *Kisses her.*
Phillis
There’s no contradicting you. How closely you argue, Tom!
Tom
And will closer, in due time. But I must hasten with this letter, to hasten towards the possession of you. Then, Phillis, consider how I must be revenged, look to it, of all your skittishness, shy looks, and at best but coy compliances.

===Chinese===
菲利斯
唉，我能信你就好了！
汤姆
口说的话不信，就信我嘴唇的触碰吧。*吻她。*
菲利斯
真没法驳你。汤姆，你这论证多紧密！
汤姆
到时辰一到，还要更紧密哩。不过这封信得赶紧送去，好赶紧把你娶到手。到那时，菲利斯，你想想我该怎么报仇——给我当心着——你那一路的轻佻、躲闪的眼色、充其量不过半推半就的应承，我都要一一清算。

===Original===
Phillis
Oh, Tom, you grow wanton, and sensual, as my lady calls it; I must not endure it. Oh! foh! you are a man﻿—an odious, filthy, male creature﻿—you should behave, if you had a right sense or were a man of sense, like Mr. Cimberton, with distance and indifference; or, let me see, some other becoming hard word, with seeming in-in-inadvertency, and not rush on one as if you were seizing a prey.﻿—But hush! the ladies are coming.﻿—Good Tom, don’t kiss me above once, and be gone. Lard, we have been fooling and toying, and not considered the main business of our masters and mistresses.
Tom
Why, their business is to be fooling and toying as soon as the parchments are ready.
Phillis
Well remembered, parchments; my lady, to my knowledge, is preparing writings between her coxcomb cousin, Cimberton, and my mistress, though my master has an eye to the parchments already prepared between your master, Mr. Bevil, and my mistress; and, I believe, my mistress herself has signed and sealed, in her heart, to Mr. Myrtle.﻿—Did I not bid you kiss me but once, and be gone? But I know you won’t be satisfied.
Tom
No, you smooth creature, how should I? *Kissing her hand.*

===Chinese===
菲利斯
噢，汤姆，你越来越放肆、越来越肉欲了——我们夫人就是这么说的；这个我可不能容。噢！呸！你是个男人——一个可憎、腌臜的雄物——你若真有体统，或算个有见识的人，就该学辛伯顿先生（Mr. Cimberton）那样，保持距离、淡漠处之；或者，让我想想，还有什么别的体面的艰深字眼——摆出一副漫—漫—漫不经心的样子，别像扑夺猎物似的朝人扑上来。——嘘！小姐们来了。——好汤姆，吻我至多一回，就走吧。天哪，咱们只顾调笑厮闹，倒把主人小姐们的大事撇在脑后了。
汤姆
嗐，他们的大事，就是等契纸一备齐，也来调笑厮闹罢了。
菲利斯
亏你记得牢，契纸；据我所知，我们夫人正在办她那位纨绔表亲辛伯顿与我们小姐之间的文契；虽说我们老爷还盯着你主人贝维尔先生与我们小姐之间早备好的那份契纸；而且我信，我们小姐自己在心里，早已对默特尔先生（Mr. Myrtle）签押用印了。——我不是吩咐你吻一回就走吗？可我知道你断不肯罢休。
汤姆
不罢休，你这光滑的人儿，教我怎么罢休？*吻她的手。*

===Original===
Phillis
Well, since you are so humble, or so cool, as to ravish my hand only, I’ll take my leave of you like a great lady, and you a man of quality. *They salute formally.*
Tom
Pox of all this state. *Offers to kiss her more closely.*
Phillis
No, prithee, Tom, mind your business. We must follow that interest which will take, but endeavour at that which will be most for us, and we like most. Oh, here is my young mistress! ***Tom** taps her neck behind, and kisses his fingers.* Go, ye liquorish fool.
*Exit **Tom**.*
*Enter **Lucinda**.*

===Chinese===
菲利斯
好吧，既然你这般谦恭，或者这般冷静，只消受我的手，我便像位贵妇人似的与你道别，你也做位有身份的绅士。*二人郑重见礼。*
汤姆
这套虚礼真该死。*欲更亲近地吻她。*
菲利斯
别，求你了汤姆，管你的正事。咱们得跟着能成事的那条线走，可也得奔着于咱们最有利、咱们最中意的那条使劲。噢，我们小姐来了！***汤姆**在她颈后轻拍一下，吻自己的手指。*去你的，馋嘴的蠢货。
*汤姆下。*
*露辛达（Lucinda）上。*

===Original===
Lucinda
Who was that you were hurrying away?
Phillis
One that I had no mind to part with.
Lucinda
Why did you turn him away then?
Phillis
For your ladyship’s service﻿—to carry your ladyship’s letter to his master. I could hardly get the rogue away.

===Chinese===
露辛达
方才你急急忙忙打发走的是谁？
菲利斯
一个我本不想放走的人。
露辛达
那又为什么赶他走？
菲利斯
为的是侍奉小姐您——叫他把小姐您的信送去给他主人。这滑头差点儿还打发不走呢。

===Original===
Lucinda
Why, has he so little love for his master?
Phillis
No; but he hath so much love for his mistress.
Lucinda
But I thought I heard him kiss you. Why did you suffer that?
Phillis
Why, madam, we vulgar take it to be a sign of love﻿—We servants, we poor people, that have nothing but our persons to bestow or treat for, are forced to deal and bargain by way of sample, and therefore as we have no parchments or wax necessary in our agreements, we squeeze with our hands and seal with our lips, to ratify vows and promises.

===Chinese===
露辛达
怎么，他对主人的忠心就这么少？
菲利斯
不是；是他对他的女主人情意太多啦。
露辛达
可我好像听见他吻你了。你怎么容得下？
菲利斯
嗐，小姐，我们下等人把这看作情意的凭据——我们这些当差的、穷人，除了自己这个人身，没什么可赠与可议价的，只得凭样品做买卖；因此我们立约用不着契纸火漆，便用手来捏、用嘴唇来盖印，好叫誓约与许诺作数。

===Original===
Lucinda
But can’t you trust one another without such earnest down?
Phillis
We don’t think it safe, any more than you gentry, to come together without deeds executed.
Lucinda
Thou art a pert merry hussy.
Phillis
I wish, madam, your lover and you were as happy as Tom and your servant are.

===Chinese===
露辛达
可没有这般定钱作押，你们就信不过彼此么？
菲利斯
我们同你们贵人们一样，觉得不立下正契便凑作一处，总不稳妥。
露辛达
你这张嘴可真伶俐轻狂。
菲利斯
小姐，但愿您的情郎和您，也同汤姆跟您这丫头一样快活。

===Original===
Lucinda
You grow impertinent.
Phillis
I have done, madam; and I won’t ask you what you intend to do with Mr. Myrtle, what your father will do with Mr. Bevil, nor what you all, especially my lady, mean by admitting Mr. Cimberton as particularly here as if he were married to you already; nay, you are married actually as far as people of quality are.
Lucinda
How is that?
Phillis
You have different beds in the same house.

===Chinese===
露辛达
你越发放肆了。
菲利斯
我不说了，小姐；我也不问您打算怎么发落默特尔先生，令尊打算怎么发落贝维尔先生，更不问你们诸位——尤其是我们夫人——安的什么心，把辛伯顿先生当自家人似的往里让，就仿佛他已经娶了您似的；说真的，照贵人们的规矩，你们其实已经算结了婚了。
露辛达
这话怎么讲？
菲利斯
你们在同一所宅子里各睡各的床。

===Original===
Lucinda
Pshaw! I have a very great value for Mr. Bevil, but have absolutely put an end to his pretensions in the letter I gave you for him. But my father, in his heart, still has a mind to him, were it not for this woman they talk of; and I am apt to imagine he is married to her, or never designs to marry at all.
Phillis
Then Mr. Myrtle﻿—
Lucinda
He had my parents’ leave to apply to me, and by that he has won me and my affections; who is to have this body of mine without them, it seems, is nothing to me. My mother says ’tis indecent for me to let my thoughts stray about the person of my husband; nay, she says a maid, rigidly virtuous, though she may have been where her lover was a thousand times, should not have made observations enough to know him from another man when she sees him in a third place.
Phillis
That is more than the severity of a nun, for not to see when one may is hardly possible; not to see when one can’t is very easy. At this rate, madam, there are a great many whom you have not seen who﻿—

===Chinese===
露辛达
啐！我对贝维尔先生极有好感，可在方才托你转交的那封信里，我已断然了结了他的指望。只是我父亲心里仍属意于他，若不是为了他们传说的那个女人；我倒倾向于想，他不是已同她结了婚，就是压根儿不打算结婚。
菲利斯
那么默特尔先生——
露辛达
他得了爹娘的许可向我求亲，凭这一条，他已赢得了我和我的情意；至于谁要连情意带身子一起娶我，似乎与我毫不相干。我母亲说，让心思在丈夫身上流连，有失体统；她还说，一个严守闺训的少女，纵然到过情人所在之处一千回，也不该观察得那样细，以致在第三个地方见着他时，能把他从别的男人里认出来。
菲利斯
这可比修女的清规还严了；能看的时候不看，几乎办不到；不能看的时候不看，那才容易。照这个规矩，小姐，天下多的是您没见过的人，他们——

===Original===
Lucinda
Mamma says the first time you see your husband should be at that instant he is made so. When your father, with the help of the minister, gives you to him, then you are to see him; then you are to observe and take notice of him; because then you are to obey him.
Phillis
But does not my lady remember you are to love as well as obey?
Lucinda
To love is a passion, it is a desire, and we must have no desires.﻿—Oh, I cannot endure the reflection! With what insensibility on my part, with what more than patience have I been exposed and offered to some awkward booby or other in every county of Great Britain!
Phillis
Indeed, madam, I wonder I never heard you speak of it before with this indignation.

===Chinese===
露辛达
妈妈说，你头一回见你丈夫，应当就在他做成你丈夫的那一刻。等你父亲借牧师之手把你交给他，那时你才许见他；那时你才许端详他、留意他；因为从那时起你须服从他。
菲利斯
可夫人难道不记得，您还得爱，不只是服从？
露辛达
爱是一种激情，是一种欲念，而我们是不许有欲念的。——噢，想起这些我就忍无可忍！我抱着怎样的麻木、怎样的超乎寻常的耐性，被陈列出来、推销给大不列颠每一郡的某个蠢笨公子或别个！
菲利斯
说真的小姐，怪了，从前竟没听您带着这样的愤懑说起过。

===Original===
Lucinda
Every corner of the land has presented me with a wealthy coxcomb. As fast as one treaty has gone off, another has come on, till my name and person have been the tittle-tattle of the whole town. What is this world come to?﻿—no shame left﻿—to be bartered for like the beasts of the field, and that in such an instance as coming together to an entire familiarity and union of soul and body. Oh! and this without being so much as well-wishers to each other, but for increase of fortune.
Phillis
But, madam, all these vexations will end very soon in one for all. Mr. Cimberton is your mother’s kinsman, and three hundred years an older gentleman than any lover you ever had; for which reason, with that of his prodigious large estate, she is resolved on him, and has sent to consult the lawyers accordingly; nay, has (whether you know it or no) been in treaty with Sir Geoffry, who, to join in the settlement, has accepted of a sum to do it, and is every moment expected in town for that purpose.
Lucinda
How do you get all this intelligence?
Phillis
By an art I have, I thank my stars, beyond all the waiting-maids in Great Britain﻿—the art of listening, madam, for your ladyship’s service.

===Chinese===
露辛达
这地方的每一个角落，都向我进献过一位有钱的纨绔。一门亲事刚吹，另一门又起，直闹得我的名字和我这个人成了满城的谈资。这世道成了什么样子？——廉耻荡然无存——竟像田间的牲口一样被人议价交易，而且是在这样一件事上：两相结合，达到灵魂与身体的彻底亲密。噢！还彼此连好意都谈不上，只为增益家财。
菲利斯
不过小姐，这些烦恼很快就要一了百了。辛伯顿先生是夫人的亲戚，做绅士比您历任情人中的任何一位都要年长三百岁；为此——再加上他那份了不得的大家业——夫人拿定了主意要他，已经遣人去请教律师们了；不但如此，她（不管您知道不知道）早已同杰弗里爵士（Sir Geoffry）接洽过了；爵士为着肯入契，已收下一笔款项，随时都会为这事进城来。
露辛达
这些消息你是怎么打探来的？
菲利斯
托众星的福，我有一门本事，全大不列颠的侍女谁也比不上——偷听的功夫，小姐，专为侍奉您。

===Original===
Lucinda
I shall soon know as much as you do; leave me, leave me, Phillis, begone. Here, here! I’ll turn you out. My mother says I must not converse with my servants, though I must converse with no one else.
*Exit **Phillis**.*
—How unhappy are we who are born to great fortunes! No one looks at us with indifference, or acts towards us on the foot of plain dealing; yet, by all I have been heretofore offered to or treated for I have been used with the most agreeable of all abuses﻿—flattery. But now, by this phlegmatic fool I’m used as nothing, or a mere thing. He, forsooth, is too wise, too learned to have any regard for desires, and I know not what the learned oaf calls sentiments of love and passion﻿—Here he comes with my mother﻿—It’s much if he looks at me, or if he does, takes no more notice of me than of any other movable in the room.
*Enter **Mrs. Sealand**, and **Mr. Cimberton**.*
Mrs. Sealand
How do I admire this noble, this learned taste of yours, and the worthy regard you have to our own ancient and honourable house in consulting a means to keep the blood as pure and as regularly descended as may be.
Cimberton

===Chinese===
露辛达
我不久就会知道得同你一样多了；去吧，去吧，菲利斯，走开。去，去！我要撵你出去了。我母亲说我不许同下人交谈，虽然除下人之外我又谁都不许交谈。
*菲利斯下。*
——生在巨富之家，我们是何等不幸！没有人以平常心看我们，也没有人以坦诚相待；然而，从前来议亲提亲的一切人手里，我受的是一切糟践中最惬意的一种——奉承。可如今，在这个黏液质的蠢货手里，我被当作无物，一件纯粹的东西。他呀，太聪明、太博学了，不屑理会什么欲念，那位博学的呆子管什么叫爱与激情的情致，我也不知道——他同我母亲来了——他肯看我一眼就算稀奇；就算看了，也不会比看屋里任何一件动产更多留意我。
*西兰夫人（Mrs. Sealand）与辛伯顿先生上。*
西兰夫人
您这高贵的、博学的趣味真令我叹服；您这般顾念我们古老而荣耀的门第，谋求一个法子让血统保持得尽可能纯净、世系尽可能规整，更是可敬。
辛伯顿

===Original===
Why, really, madam, the young women of this age are treated with discourses of such a tendency, and their imaginations so bewildered in flesh and blood, that a man of reason can’t talk to be understood. They have no ideas of happiness, but what are more gross than the gratification of hunger and thirst.
Lucinda
With how much reflection he is a coxcomb! *Aside.*
Cimberton
And in truth, madam, I have considered it as a most brutal custom that persons of the first character in the world should go as ordinarily, and with as little shame, to bed as to dinner with one another. They proceed to the propagation of the species as openly as to the preservation of the individual.
Lucinda
She that willingly goes to bed to thee must have no shame, I’m sure. *Aside.*
Mrs. Sealand

===Chinese===
唉，说真的，夫人，如今调教年轻姑娘们用的全是这一路言论，她们的想象力又尽在血肉里打转，以致讲理性的人说起话来反叫人听不懂。她们对幸福别无概念，有的概念也比饥渴的餍足更粗鄙。
露辛达
他做纨绔做得多么有思虑！*旁白。*
辛伯顿
而且说实在的，夫人，我一向认为这是一桩极野蛮的风俗：世上头等身份的人物，竟同吃晚饭一样家常、一样不知羞地彼此上床就寝。他们传宗接代，同保全自身一样公开。
露辛达
甘愿同你上床的女人，必定是不知道羞耻的，我敢肯定。*旁白。*
西兰夫人

===Original===
Oh, cousin Cimberton! cousin Cimberton! how abstracted, how refined is your sense of things! But, indeed, it is too true there is nothing so ordinary as to say, in the best governed families, my master and lady have gone to bed; one does not know but it might have been said of one’s self. *Hiding her face with her fan.*
Cimberton
Lycurgus, madam, instituted otherwise; among the Lacedaemonians the whole female world was pregnant, but none but the mothers themselves knew by whom; their meetings were secret, and the amorous congress always by stealth; and no such professed doings between the sexes as are tolerated among us under the audacious word, marriage.
Mrs. Sealand
Oh, had I lived in those days and been a matron of Sparta, one might with less indecency have had ten children, according to that modest institution, than one, under the confusion of our modern, barefaced manner.
Lucinda
And yet, poor woman, she has gone through the whole ceremony, and here I stand a melancholy proof of it. *Aside.*
Mrs. Sealand

===Chinese===
噢，辛伯顿表亲！辛伯顿表亲！您对事物的见识何等玄虚、何等精微！不过说真的，确是如此：再没有比这更寻常的话了——在顶有规矩的人家，人们也照样说：老爷和夫人已经安寝了；谁知道这话会不会也曾说到自己头上呢。*以扇遮面。*
辛伯顿
吕库古（Lycurgus）立法可不然，夫人；在拉克代蒙人中间，整个女性世界都怀了孕，可除了母亲们自己，谁也不知道孩子的父亲是谁；他们的相会隐秘，欢爱的结合总是偷偷摸摸；绝没有像我们中间这样，在“婚姻”这个放肆字眼的庇护下男女公开行之的那等勾当。
西兰夫人
噢，倘使我生在那些岁月、做斯巴达的主妇，照那知耻的制度养下十个孩子，也比照我们如今这不顾脸面的路数养一个，少些不雅。
露辛达
然而，可怜的女人，她全副仪节一样不落都行过了，我不就站在这儿，做个愁人的明证么。*旁白。*
西兰夫人

===Original===
We will talk then of business. That girl walking about the room there is to be your wife. She has, I confess, no ideas, no sentiments, that speak her born of a thinking mother.
Cimberton
I have observed her; her lively look, free air, and disengaged countenance speak her very﻿—
Lucinda
Very what?
Cimberton
If you please, madam﻿—to set her a little that way.
Mrs. Sealand

===Chinese===
那便谈正事吧。在那边屋里踱来踱去的姑娘，便是您的妻子了。我承认，她没有什么见识、什么情致，足以显出她生自一位会思考的母亲。
辛伯顿
我观察过她了；她活泼的眼神、自在的风度、无拘的面色，都显出她非常——
露辛达
非常什么？
辛伯顿
如您允许，夫人——好使她朝那个方向调理调理。
西兰夫人

===Original===
Lucinda, say nothing to him, you are not a match for him; when you are married, you may speak to such a husband when you’re spoken to. But I am disposing of you above yourself every way.
Cimberton
Madam, you cannot but observe the inconveniences I expose myself to, in hopes that your ladyship will be the consort of my better part. As for the young woman, she is rather an impediment than a help to a man of letters and speculation. Madam, there is no reflection, no philosophy, can at all times subdue the sensitive life, but the animal shall sometimes carry away the man. Ha! ay, the vermilion of her lips.
Lucinda
Pray, don’t talk of me thus.
Cimberton
The pretty enough﻿—pant of her bosom.
Lucinda

===Chinese===
露辛达，别跟他说话，你不是他的对手；等你嫁了人，这样的丈夫向你开口时，你才可以答话。不过我这是把你许配得处处高攀了。
辛伯顿
夫人，您不会看不出，我这是自甘种种不便，指望您家小姐做我这更高一半的配偶。至于这位年轻姑娘，对于一位文人、一位思想家，她毋宁是个累赘而非帮手。夫人，任何深思、任何哲学，都不能时时降伏这感性生命，兽性有时是会把人携带着跑的。哈！是的，她嘴唇的朱红。
露辛达
求您了，别这样谈论我。
辛伯顿
满标致的——她胸脯的起伏。
露辛达

===Original===
Sir! madam, don’t you hear him?
Cimberton
Her forward chest.
Lucinda
Intolerable!
Cimberton
High health.
Lucinda

===Chinese===
先生！妈妈，您没听见他的话吗？
辛伯顿
她前挺的胸膛。
露辛达
难以容忍！
辛伯顿
健旺的血色。
露辛达

===Original===
The grave, easy impudence of him!
Cimberton
Proud heart.
Lucinda
Stupid coxcomb!
Cimberton
I say, madam, her impatience, while we are looking at her, throws out all attractions﻿—her arms﻿—her neck﻿—what a spring in her step!
Lucinda

===Chinese===
瞧他这一本正经、从容不迫的厚脸皮！
辛伯顿
高傲的心。
露辛达
愚蠢的纨绔！
辛伯顿
我说呀，夫人，我们打量她时她那按捺不住的性子，反倒把百般魅力一齐抛洒出来——她的手臂——她的颈项——她脚步里带着怎样一股弹性！
露辛达

===Original===
Don’t you run me over thus, you strange unaccountable!
Cimberton
What an elasticity in her veins and arteries!
Lucinda
I have no veins, no arteries.
Mrs. Sealand
Oh, child! hear him, he talks finely; he’s a scholar, he knows what you have.
Cimberton

===Chinese===
别这样把我从头到脚数落过去，你这个莫名其妙的东西！
辛伯顿
她的静脉动脉里含着怎样的弹力！
露辛达
我没有静脉，也没有动脉。
西兰夫人
噢，孩子！听他说，他说得多好；他是位学者，你身上有什么他都知道。
辛伯顿

===Original===
The speaking invitation of her shape, the gathering of herself up, and the indignation you see in the pretty little thing﻿—Now, I am considering her, on this occasion, but as one that is to be pregnant.
Lucinda
The familiar, learned, unseasonable puppy! *Aside.*
Cimberton
And pregnant undoubtedly she will be yearly. I fear I shan’t, for many years, have discretion enough to give her one fallow season.
Lucinda
Monster! there’s no bearing it. The hideous sot! there’s no enduring it, to be thus surveyed like a steed at sale.
Cimberton

===Chinese===
她身段那会说话的邀请，她全身的收束挺立，还有你这小人儿一脸的嗔怒——眼下在这场合，我只把她当作一个将要受孕的人来考量。
露辛达
好个亲昵、博学、不合时宜的小狗才！*旁白。*
辛伯顿
而且毫无疑问，她将年年受孕。我怕的是，多少年之内我都不见得有足够的节制，肯给她一个休耕的季节。
露辛达
怪物！叫人忍无可忍。可憎的蠢货！被人这样像待售的骏马一样打量，再没有比这更难堪的了。
辛伯顿

===Original===
At sale! She’s very illiterate﻿—But she’s very well limbed too; turn her in; I see what she is.
*Exit **Lucinda**, in a rage.*
Mrs. Sealand
Go, you creature, I am ashamed of you.
Cimberton
No harm done﻿—you know, madam, the better sort of people, as I observed to you, treat by their lawyers of weddings *adjusting himself at the glass*﻿—and the woman in the bargain, like the mansion house in the sale of the estate, is thrown in, and what that is, whether good or bad, is not at all considered.
Mrs. Sealand
I grant it; and therefore make no demand for her youth and beauty, and every other accomplishment, as the common world think ’em, because she is not polite.

===Chinese===
待售！她太没学问了——不过她四肢也生得很端正；带她转个身；我看清她是什么成色了。
*露辛达怒下。*
西兰夫人
走开，你这东西，真给我丢脸。
辛伯顿
并没伤着谁——您知道的，夫人，体面人家，我先前也对您说过，婚事都是凭律师们议办的*对着镜子整理仪容*——那女人也是搭头，如同卖田庄时搭上的宅邸，白送了事；至于这搭头是好是坏，压根儿无人考虑。
西兰夫人
这话我认；因此我也不拿她的青春美貌以及寻常世人看重的种种才艺讨价，因为她并不讲究文雅。

===Original===
Cimberton
Madam, I know your exalted understanding, abstracted, as it is, from vulgar prejudices, will not be offended, when I declare to you, I marry to have an heir to my estate, and not to beget a colony, or a plantation. This young woman’s beauty and constitution will demand provision for a tenth child at least.
Mrs. Sealand
With all that wit and learning, how considerate! What an economist! *Aside.*﻿—Sir, I cannot make her any other than she is; or say she is much better than the other young women of this age, or fit for much besides being a mother; but I have given directions for the marriage settlements, and Sir Geoffry Cimberton’s counsel is to meet ours here, at this hour, concerning this joining in the deed, which, when executed, makes you capable of settling what is due to Lucinda’s fortune. Herself, as I told you, I say nothing of.
Cimberton
No, no, no, indeed, madam, it is not usual; and I must depend upon my own reflection and philosophy not to overstock my family.
Mrs. Sealand
I cannot help her, cousin Cimberton; but she is, for aught I see, as well as the daughter of anybody else.

===Chinese===
辛伯顿
夫人，我知道您那超拔的理解力既已超脱俗众的偏见，便不会见怪，容我向您言明：我结婚是为给我的家产生一位继承人，不是要生出一族殖民，或一座种植园。这位年轻姑娘的美貌与体质，至少会要求给第十个孩子备下财产。
西兰夫人
有这等的才思和学问，多会体贴！好一位精打细算的人！*旁白。*——先生，我不能把她变作别样的人；也不能说她比时下别的年轻姑娘们好得多，或说除了做母亲之外还堪当什么大用；不过婚产文契我已吩咐拟好，杰弗里·辛伯顿爵士的律师约定此时在此同我们的律师会齐，商议联署契据的事；契据一签署，您便能把该归露辛达名下财产的部分定夺下来。至于她本人，我说过，我一个字也不提。
辛伯顿
不必，不必，不必，确实，夫人，这不是惯例；我只得倚仗自己的深思与哲学，免得家中人丁过剩。
西兰夫人
她的事我管不了，辛伯顿表亲；不过据我看，她同别人家的女儿一样不差。

===Original===
Cimberton
That is very true, madam.
*Enter a Servant, who whispers **Mrs. Sealand**.*
Mrs. Sealand
The lawyers are come, and now we are to hear what they have resolved as to the point whether it’s necessary that Sir Geoffry should join in the settlement, as being what they call in the remainder. But, good cousin, you must have patience with ’em. These lawyers, I am told, are of a different kind; one is what they call a chamber counsel, the other a pleader. The conveyancer is slow, from an imperfection in his speech, and therefore shunned the bar, but extremely passionate and impatient of contradiction. The other is as warm as he; but has a tongue so voluble, and a head so conceited, he will suffer nobody to speak but himself.
Cimberton
You mean old Serjeant Target and Counsellor Bramble? I have heard of ’em.
Mrs. Sealand
The same. Show in the gentlemen.

===Chinese===
辛伯顿
这话千真万确，夫人。
*一仆人上，与西兰夫人耳语。*
西兰夫人
律师们到了，如今咱们就要听听他们对这一点的裁断：杰弗里爵士是否必须联名入契——因为用他们的话说，他是“剩余继承”中的人。不过，好表亲，您得容让他们些。听说这两位律师不是一路人：一位是所谓坐堂顾问，一位是出庭讼师。那位立契律师口齿有毛病，说话慢，因此回避了出庭一途，性子却极火爆，受不得半句反驳。另一位同他一样急躁；只是那条舌头太伶俐，那颗脑袋太自负，除他自己之外谁也不许开口。
辛伯顿
您是说老塔吉特（Serjeant Target）高级律师和布兰布尔（Counsellor Bramble）律师吧？我听说过他们。
西兰夫人
正是他们。请两位先生进来。

===Original===
*Exit Servant.*
*Reenter Servant, introducing **Myrtle** and **Tom** disguised as **Bramble** and **Target**.*
Mrs. Sealand
Gentlemen, this is the party concerned, Mr. Cimberton; and I hope you have considered of the matter.
Target
Yes, madam, we have agreed that it must be by indent﻿—dent﻿—dent﻿—dent﻿—
Bramble
Yes, madam, Mr. Serjeant and myself have agreed, as he is pleased to inform you, that it must be an indenture tripartite,30 and tripartite let it be, for Sir Geoffry must needs be a party; old Cimberton, in the year 1619, says, in that ancient roll in Mr. Serjeant’s hands, as recourse thereto being had, will more at large appear﻿—

===Chinese===
*仆人下。*
*仆人复上，引默特尔与汤姆入，二人假扮作布兰布尔与塔吉特。*
西兰夫人
两位先生，这位便是当事的一方，辛伯顿先生；我想你们已把这事斟酌过了。
塔吉特
是，夫人，我们议定了，这事必得凭契—契—契—契——
布兰布尔
正是，夫人，塔吉特高级律师与我本人已商定——承他好意告知诸位——此事必得订立三方契据，30 而且就依三方订立，因为杰弗里爵士务必列作一方；老辛伯顿于 1619 年，塔吉特高级律师手中那卷古老案卷有云——参阅该卷即知——详情将于彼处更详明地显现——

===Original===
Target
Yes, and by the deeds in your hands, it appears that﻿—
Bramble
Mr. Serjeant, I beg of you to make no inferences upon what is in our custody; but speak to the titles in your own deeds. I shall not show that deed till my client is in town.
Cimberton
You know best your own methods.
Mrs. Sealand
The single question is, whether the entail is such that my cousin, Sir Geoffry, is necessary in this affair?

===Chinese===
塔吉特
正是，而且凭你手中的契据，可见——
布兰布尔
塔吉特律师，恳请您别对我们保管的东西妄下推论；还是只谈您自己契据里的产权吧。在我的当事人进城之前，我那件契据是不会出示的。
辛伯顿
你们的手续你们自己最清楚。
西兰夫人
唯一的问题是：这限定继承是否牵涉到我表亲杰弗里爵士，叫他非联名入契不可？

===Original===
Bramble
Yes, as to the lordship of Tretriplet, but not as to the messuage of Grimgribber.
Target
I say that Gr﻿—gr﻿—that Gr﻿—gr﻿—Grimgribber, Grimgribber is in us; that is to say the remainder thereof, as well as that of Tr﻿—tr﻿—Triplet.
Bramble
You go upon the deed of Sir Ralph, made in the middle of the last century, precedent to that in which old Cimberton made over the remainder, and made it pass to the heirs general, by which your client comes in; and I question whether the remainder even of Tretriplet is in him﻿—But we are willing to waive that, and give him a valuable consideration. But we shall not purchase what is in us forever, as Grimgribber is, at the rate, as we guard against the contingent of Mr. Cimberton having no son﻿—Then we know Sir Geoffry is the first of the collateral male line in this family﻿—yet﻿—
Target
Sir, Gr﻿—gr﻿—ber is﻿—

===Chinese===
布兰布尔
是，就特特里普利特（Tretriplet）领地而言是这样；但就格里姆格里伯（Grimgribber）宅邸而言则不然。
塔吉特
我说那格—格——那格—格——格里姆格里伯，格里姆格里伯在我们手里；就是说，其剩余继承权，同特—特—特里普利特的一样，都在我们手里。
布兰布尔
您依据的是拉尔夫爵士（Sir Ralph）那件契据，立于上世纪中叶，先于老辛伯顿让渡剩余权的那件，且使产权传于一般继承人，您的当事人便是凭这一层进来的；我且怀疑，连特特里普利特的剩余权是否真在他手里——不过我们愿意不究这一层，给他一笔相当的对价。但像格里姆格里伯这样已归我们所有的永久产权，我们是不会照那个价钱买的——既然要提防辛伯顿先生无子这一或有之虞——再者，我们知道杰弗里爵士是这一族旁系男支里的第一人——然而——
塔吉特
先生，格—格—伯是——

===Original===
Bramble
I apprehend you very well, and your argument might be of force, and we would be inclined to hear that in all its parts﻿—But, sir, I see very plainly what you are going into. I tell you, it is as probable a contingent that Sir Geoffry may die before Mr. Cimberton, as that he may outlive him.
Target
Sir, we are not ripe for that yet, but I must say﻿—
Bramble
Sir, I allow you the whole extent of that argument; but that will go no farther than as to the claimants under old Cimberton. I am of opinion that, according to the instruction of Sir Ralph, he could not dock the entail, and then create a new estate for the heirs general.
Target
Sir, I have not patience to be told that, when Gr﻿—gr﻿—ber﻿—

===Chinese===
布兰布尔
我完全明白您的意思，您的论证未尝没有力量，我们也未尝不愿从头到尾听个周详——可是先生，您要扯到哪里去，我看得一清二楚。我告诉您，杰弗里爵士先辛伯顿先生而死，与他比辛伯顿先生更长寿，这两种可能不相上下。
塔吉特
先生，那层还没议到火候，可我必须说——
布兰布尔
先生，那套论证的全幅道理我都让给您；只是它至多涉及老辛伯顿名下的诸权利人。依我之见，遵照拉尔夫爵士的指示，他不能废止限定继承，再另创一份产业给一般继承人。
塔吉特
先生，听人讲这个我可没耐性，那格—格—伯——

===Original===
Bramble
I will allow it you, Mr. Serjeant; but there must be the word heirs forever, to make such an estate as you pretend.
Cimberton
I must be impartial, though you are counsel for my side of the question. Were it not that you are so good as to allow him what he has not said, I should think it very hard you should answer him without hearing him﻿—But, gentlemen, I believe you have both considered this matter, and are firm in your different opinions. ’Twere better, therefore, you proceeded according to the particular sense of each of you, and gave your thoughts distinctly in writing. And do you see, sirs, pray let me have a copy of what you say in English.
Bramble
Why, what is all we have been saying? In English! Oh! but I forget myself, you’re a wit. But, however, to please you, sir, you shall have it, in as plain terms as the law will admit of.
Cimberton
But I would have it, sir, without delay.

===Chinese===
布兰布尔
我让给您便是，塔吉特律师；可要成您所主张的那种产权，契上必得有“继承人永享”的字样。
辛伯顿
我必须秉公而论，虽然你们是我这一方的律师。若不是您好心替他说了他不曾说的话，我简直要说：不听他讲完便作答，未免太强横——不过，两位先生，我相信你们都已斟酌过此事，各自的意见也拿得牢靠。那么不如各按各的见解行事，把你们的意思分别写明。还有，两位可听真了：请给我一份你们所讲内容的英文文本。
布兰布尔
嗐，我们方才讲的不都是英文么？还要英文文本！噢！瞧我忘了自己是谁了——您是位才子。不过也罢，为讨您欢心，先生，您会拿到的，措辞浅显得法律所能容许的地步。
辛伯顿
可我要它即刻就来，先生，不得耽搁。

===Original===
Bramble
That, sir, the law will not admit of. The Courts are sitting at Westminster, and I am this moment obliged to be at every one of them, and ’twould be wrong if I should not be in the hall to attend one of ’em at least; the rest would take it ill else. Therefore, I must leave what I have said to Mr. Serjeant’s consideration, and I will digest his arguments on my part, and you shall hear from me again, sir.
*Exit **Bramble**.*
Target
Agreed, agreed.
Cimberton
Mr. Bramble is very quick; he parted a little abruptly.
Target
He could not bear my argument; I pinched him to the quick about that Gr﻿—gr﻿—ber.

===Chinese===
布兰布尔
这一层，先生，法律可容不得。威斯敏斯特各法院正在开庭，我此刻便须赶到每一处去；若不到法庭大堂里至少侍候一处，那就是我的不是了；否则其余各处会见怪的。因此，我方才所讲的，只得留请塔吉特律师斟酌，他那套论证也由我这边来消化，先生，您且候我的回话。
*布兰布尔下。*
塔吉特
同意，同意。
辛伯顿
布兰布尔先生真是性急；辞别得有些仓促。
塔吉特
他受不住我的论证；在那桩格—格—伯的事上，我掐中了他的痛处。

===Original===
Mrs. Sealand
I saw that, for he durst not so much as hear you. I shall send to you, Mr. Serjeant, as soon as Sir Geoffry comes to town, and then I hope all may be adjusted.
Target
I shall be at my chambers, at my usual hours.
*Exit.*
Cimberton
Madam, if you please, I’ll now attend you to the tea table, where I shall hear from your ladyship reason and good sense, after all this law and gibberish.
Mrs. Sealand

===Chinese===
西兰夫人
这我看得出来，他连听都不敢听您讲呢。杰弗里爵士一进城，我便遣人请您，塔吉特律师；那时望一切都能定妥。
塔吉特
我在律师事务室里，按惯常的时辰恭候。
*下。*
辛伯顿
夫人，如您允许，我这就陪您到茶桌旁去；听罢这一通法律呓语，也该听听夫人的理性与卓识了。
西兰夫人

===Original===
’Tis a wonderful thing, sir, that men of professions do not study to talk the substance of what they have to say in the language of the rest of the world. Sure, they’d find their account in it.
Cimberton
They might, perhaps, madam, with people of your good sense; but with the generality ’twould never do. The vulgar would have no respect for truth and knowledge, if they were exposed to naked view.
Truth is too simple, of all art bereaved:
Since the world will﻿—why let it be deceived.
*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
先生，有职业的人不肯用心把要说的事实用世上旁人的语言讲出来，真是咄咄怪事。这样做了，他们准保有好处。
辛伯顿
同夫人这样有见识的人，或许成；但对芸芸众生，断然行不通。真理与知识若赤裸裸示于人前，庸众便再不会敬畏它们。
真理太简单，剥尽一切机巧：
世人既要如此——就由它受骗去吧。
*众人下。*

## Act I / 第一幕

===Original===
Scene I. Sir John Bevil’s House.
*Enter **Sir John Bevil** and **Humphry**.*
John Bevil
Have you ordered that I should not be interrupted while I am dressing?
Humphry
Yes, sir; I believed you had something of moment to say to me.
John Bevil
Let me see, Humphry; I think it is now full forty years since I first took thee to be about myself.
Humphry
I thank you, sir, it has been an easy forty years; and I have passed ’em without much sickness, care, or labour.
John Bevil
Thou hast a brave constitution; you are a year or two older than I am, sirrah.

===Chinese===
第一场　约翰·贝维尔爵士宅。
*约翰·贝维尔爵士（Sir John Bevil）与汉弗莱（Humphry）上。*
约翰·贝维尔
你吩咐过了吗——我更衣的时候不许任何人打扰？
汉弗莱
吩咐过了，老爷；我想您有要紧话要对我说。
约翰·贝维尔
我算算看，汉弗莱；从我把你带进身边伺候，到如今怕是足足四十年了。
汉弗莱
多谢老爷，这四十年过得安逸；我没怎么生过病、操过心、受过累，就这么过来了。
约翰·贝维尔
你这身子骨真是结实；小伙子，你比我还年长一两岁呢。

===Original===
Humphry
You have ever been of that mind, sir.
John Bevil
You knave, you know it; I took thee for thy gravity and sobriety, in my wild years.
Humphry
Ah, sir! our manners were formed from our different fortunes, not our different age. Wealth gave a loose to your youth, and poverty put a restraint upon mine.
John Bevil
Well, Humphry, you know I have been a kind master to you; I have used you, for the ingenuous nature I observed in you from the beginning, more like an humble friend than a servant.

===Chinese===
汉弗莱
您向来都是这个说法，老爷。
约翰·贝维尔
你这滑头，你自己心里明白；我当年放浪的时候，正是看中你的持重老成才把你收下的。
汉弗莱
嗐，老爷！咱们的性子是各人的财运塑出来的，不是各人的年纪。富贵放纵了您的青春，贫贱拘束了我的。
约翰·贝维尔
好了汉弗莱，你知道我待你一向不薄；我打头一眼就看出你天性坦直，因此待你与其说像个仆人，倒不如说像个谦卑的朋友。

===Original===
Humphry
I humbly beg you’ll be so tender of me as to explain your commands, sir, without any farther preparation.
John Bevil
I’ll tell thee, then: In the first place, this wedding of my son’s in all probability﻿—shut the door﻿—will never be at all.
Humphry
How, sir! not be at all? for what reason is it carried on in appearance?
John Bevil
Honest Humphry, have patience; and I’ll tell thee all in order. I have, myself, in some part of my life, lived (indeed) with freedom, but, I hope, without reproach. Now, I thought liberty would be as little injurious to my son; therefore, as soon as he grew towards man, I indulged him in living after his own manner. I knew not how, otherwise, to judge of his inclination; for what can be concluded from a behaviour under restraint and fear?15 But what charms me above all expression is, that my son has never, in the least action, the most distant hint or word, valued himself upon that great estate of his mother’s, which, according to our marriage settlement, he has had ever since he came to age.
Humphry
No, sir; on the contrary, he seems afraid of appearing to enjoy it, before you or any belonging to you. He is as dependent and resigned to your will as if he had not a farthing but what must come from your immediate bounty. You have ever acted like a good and generous father, and he like an obedient and grateful son.

===Chinese===
汉弗莱
小的求老爷体恤，有什么吩咐就直说吧，别再这么层层铺垫了。
约翰·贝维尔
那我便告诉你：头一件，我儿子这门婚事，十有八九——把门关上——根本办不成了。
汉弗莱
怎么，老爷！办不成了？那为什么表面上还照旧操办？
约翰·贝维尔
老实的汉弗莱，别急；我原原本本讲给你听。我自己平生有一段日子，确实也活得自在，但我想还不致落人话柄。我想，自在对我儿子也不至于有什么妨害；所以他一到成年人的年纪，我便任凭他照自己的性子过活。不如此，我没法判断他的心性；因为一副受拘束、怀畏惧的行为里，能看出什么来呢？15 可有一件事最叫我心醉，简直没法形容：我儿子从未有一星半点的举动、暗示或言辞，拿他母亲那宗大家产自矜——按我们的婚产契约，他一成年那产业便归了他。
汉弗莱
是没有，老爷；不但如此，在您和您跟前任何人面前，他仿佛还怕露出坐享其产的样子。他事事仰承您的意旨，仿佛自己一个子儿也没有，全得仰仗您的恩赏过活。您行事始终像位仁厚慷慨的父亲，他也像个性顺知恩的儿子。

===Original===
John Bevil
Nay, his carriage is so easy to all with whom he converses, that he is never assuming, never prefers himself to others, nor ever is guilty of that rough sincerity which a man is not called to, and certainly disobliges most of his acquaintance; to be short, Humphry, his reputation was so fair in the world, that old Sealand, the great India merchant, has offered his only daughter, and sole heiress to that vast estate of his, as a wife for him. You may be sure I made no difficulties, the match was agreed on, and this very day named for the wedding.
Humphry
What hinders the proceeding?
John Bevil
Don’t interrupt me. You know I was last Thursday at the masquerade; my son, you may remember, soon found us out. He knew his grandfather’s habit, which I then wore; and though it was the mode, in the last age, yet the masquers, you know, followed us as if we had been the most monstrous figures in that whole assembly.
Humphry
I remember, indeed, a young man of quality in the habit of a clown, that was particularly troublesome.
John Bevil
Right; he was too much what he seemed to be. You remember how impertinently he followed and teased us, and would know who we were.

===Chinese===
约翰·贝维尔
不止如此，他待所有交往的人都那么随和，从不专断自大，从不压人一头，也从不犯那种没人请教他偏要出口的粗直——那种直性子定要开罪他大半相识；简而言之，汉弗莱，他的名声在外那么清白，连老西兰——那位印度贸易的大商人——都主动提出把独女、也是他那份庞大家业的唯一继承人，许给他为妻。你可想见我没有半点为难，亲事当场议定，婚期就定在今儿。
汉弗莱
那又是什么绊住了，办不下去？
约翰·贝维尔
别打岔。你知道上星期四我去了化装舞会；你该记得，我儿子很快就寻着了我们。他认得我那身穿的衣裳，是他祖父的旧装；那打扮虽是上个时代的时式，可你知道，那些戴假面的人偏跟着我们团团转，好像我们是全场最骇人的怪模样。
汉弗莱
我倒真记得，有位穿乡下人衣裳的贵族公子，缠人缠得格外厉害。
约翰·贝维尔
正是；他那副表里如一未免太过了。你记得他多么无礼地跟着纠缠我们，非要打听我们是谁。

===Original===
Humphry
I know he has a mind to come into that particular. *Aside.*
John Bevil
Ay, he followed us till the gentleman who led the lady in the Indian mantle presented that gay creature to the rustic, and bid him (like Cymon in the fable) grow polite by falling in love, and let that worthy old gentleman alone, meaning me. The clown was not reformed, but rudely persisted, and offered to force off my mask; with that, the gentleman, throwing off his own, appeared to be my son, and in his concern for me, tore off that of the nobleman; at this they seized each other; the company called the guards, and in the surprise the lady swooned away; upon which my son quitted his adversary, and had now no care but of the lady. When raising her in his arms, “Art thou gone,” cried he, “forever?﻿—forbid it, Heaven!” She revived at his known voice, and with the most familiar, though modest, gesture, hangs in safety over his shoulder weeping, but wept as in the arms of one before whom she could give herself a loose, were she not under observation; while she hides her face in his neck, he carefully conveys her from the company.
Humphry
I have observed this accident has dwelt upon you very strongly.
John Bevil
Her uncommon air, her noble modesty, the dignity of her person, and the occasion itself, drew the whole assembly together; and I soon heard it buzzed about she was the adopted daughter of a famous sea-officer who had served in France. Now this unexpected and public discovery of my son’s so deep concern for her﻿—
Humphry
Was what, I suppose, alarmed Mr. Sealand, in behalf of his daughter, to break off the match?
John Bevil
You are right. He came to me yesterday and said he thought himself disengaged from the bargain; being credibly informed my son was already married, or worse, to the lady at the masquerade. I palliated matters, and insisted on our agreement; but we parted with little less than a direct breach between us.16

===Chinese===
汉弗莱
我知道他就要说到那一节了。*旁白。*
约翰·贝维尔
是啊，他一路跟着我们，直到那位搀着披印度披肩的小姐的绅士，把那位姣艳的人儿引见给这乡巴佬，叫他（像寓言里的西蒙（Cymon）一样）因恋爱而变得文雅，放过那位可敬的老先生——说的就是我。那乡下人并不收敛，反而无礼地纠缠不休，竟要强扯我的假面；那绅士当即摘下自己的假面，原来正是我儿子；他情急护父，又扯下了那位贵族的假面；两人随即扭作一团；众人喊来卫兵，慌乱之中那位小姐晕了过去；我儿子撇了对手，此刻一心只有那位小姐了。他把她抱在怀里扶起来，喊道："你这就去了么——永远去了么？——上天不容！"她听出是他熟悉的声音，悠悠醒转，带着一种最亲昵却又最端庄的姿态，伏在他肩头安然哭泣——哭得就像倚在一个可以纵情一恸的人怀里，若不是众人围着看的话；她把脸埋进他的颈窝，他则小心地把她从人群中护送出去。
汉弗莱
我看得出，这桩意外一直在您心里盘桓不去。
约翰·贝维尔
她那不俗的风仪、高贵的娴静、本人的端庄气度，加上事情本身的离奇，把全场的人都引了拢来；不久我便听人纷纷传说，她是一位曾效力于法国的著名海军军官的养女。如今，我儿子对她这番深情竟这样意外地当众泄露——
汉弗莱
我猜，就是这件事叫西兰先生为着他女儿着了慌，要退掉这门亲？
约翰·贝维尔
你说得对。他昨天来见我，说他认为自己已经脱开了这桩买卖；因为他得了可靠的信，说我儿子同那位化装舞会上的小姐不是已经成婚，就是更糟的关系。我把事情遮掩了过去，坚持咱们的约定；可我们分手时，几乎等于当场决裂了。16

===Original===
Humphry
Well, sir; and what notice have you taken of all this to my young master?
John Bevil
That’s what I wanted to debate with you. I have said nothing to him yet﻿—but look you, Humphry, if there is so much in this amour of his, that he denies upon my summons to marry, I have cause enough to be offended; and then by my insisting upon his marrying today, I shall know how far he is engaged to this lady in masquerade, and from thence only shall be able to take my measures. In the meantime I would have you find out how far that rogue, his man, is let into his secret. He, I know, will play tricks as much to cross me, as to serve his master.
Humphry
Why do you think so of him, sir? I believe he is no worse than I was for you, at your son’s age.
John Bevil
I see it in the rascal’s looks. But I have dwelt on these things too long; I’ll go to my son immediately, and while I’m gone, your part is to convince his rogue, Tom, that I am in earnest.﻿—I’ll leave him to you.
*Exit **Sir John Bevil**.*
Humphry
Well, though this father and son live as well together as possible, yet their fear of giving each other pain is attended with constant mutual uneasiness. I’m sure I have enough to do to be honest, and yet keep well with them both. But they know I love ’em, and that makes the task less painful however. Oh, here’s the prince of poor coxcombs, the representative of all the better fed than taught. Ho! ho! Tom, whither so gay and so airy this morning?
*Enter **Tom**, singing.*

===Chinese===
汉弗莱
那么，老爷；这些情由您对小主人提过一个字吗？
约翰·贝维尔
这正是我要同你商量的。我至今一个字没对他说——可是你听着，汉弗莱，倘若他这桩情事当真陷得这么深，深到我传令成婚他竟敢抗命，那我就有足够的理由动怒了；而只要我咬定他今天就得成婚，我便能探出他同那位假面小姐纠缠到了什么地步，也只有从那里，我才能定下我的步骤。这期间，我要你去探探那个滑头——他的跟班——深得他多少机密。我知道，那小子耍花招，一半为讨好他主人，一半也为着跟我作对。
汉弗莱
老爷，您怎么这样看他？我信他坏不到哪儿去，同您儿子这般岁数时的我差不多。
约翰·贝维尔
我从那小子的眉眼里就看得出来。不过这些事我耽搁得太久了；我这就去我儿子那儿，趁我不在，你的差事是叫他那个滑头汤姆（Tom）相信，我这话是当真的。——他交给你了。
*约翰·贝维尔爵士下。*
汉弗莱
唉，这父子俩相处得再好不过了，可他们彼此都怕叫对方难过，反倒落得个长年互相提心吊胆。我呢，要做人诚实，又要两边都讨好，这份差事也真够我受的。好在他们知道我爱他们，这样那差事不管怎样总好受些。噢，可怜的纨绔王子来了，一切"喂养有余、教养不足"之辈的代表。嗬！嗬！汤姆，今儿早上这么神气、这么轻快，上哪儿去呀？
*汤姆上，唱着歌。*

===Original===
Tom
Sir, we servants of single gentlemen are another kind of people than you domestic ordinary drudges that do business; we are raised above you. The pleasures of board-wages, tavern dinners, and many a clear gain; vails, alas! you never heard or dreamt of.
Humphry
Thou hast follies and vices enough for a man of ten thousand a year, though ’tis but as t’other day that I sent for you to town to put you into Mr. Sealand’s family, that you might learn a little before I put you to my young master, who is too gentle for training such a rude thing as you were into proper obedience. You then pulled off your hat to everyone you met in the street, like a bashful great awkward cub as you were. But your great oaken cudgel, when you were a booby, became you much better than that dangling stick at your button, now you are a fop. That’s fit for nothing, except it hangs there to be ready for your master’s hand when you are impertinent.
Tom
Uncle Humphry, you know my master scorns to strike his servants. You talk as if the world was now just as it was when my old master and you were in your youth; when you went to dinner because it was so much o’clock, when the great blow was given in the hall at the pantry door, and all the family came out of their holes in such strange dresses and formal faces as you see in the pictures in our long gallery in the country.
Humphry
Why, you wild rogue!
Tom
You could not fall to your dinner till a formal fellow in a black gown said something over the meat, as if the cook had not made it ready enough.

===Chinese===
汤姆
老爷，我们这些伺候单身绅士的仆人，同你们这些当家吃苦的粗使家伙是两种人；我们生来就高你们一等。包饭钱的甜头、酒馆里的正餐、种种干干净净的进项——还有赏钱！唉，那些你们连听都没听过、做梦也没梦见过。
汉弗莱
你的糊涂和毛病，足够养一个年进万镑的老爷了——虽说前些日子我才把你叫进城，先送进西兰先生府里，让你学点规矩，再把你交给小主人，他性子太温和，调教不动你这样一个粗坯。那时节你见了街上任何人都要脱帽，一副又腼腆又笨拙的大孩子样。不过你做傻小子时那根大橡木棍，倒比如今你当上了花花公子、拴在扣眼上晃荡的那根小棒棒更配你。那玩意儿一无用处，只配挂在那儿，等你放肆的时候，好让你主人伸手就够得着。
汤姆
汉弗莱伯父，您知道我主人是不屑动手打仆人的。您说话的口气，好像如今的世界还是老主人和您年轻时的老样子；那时候一到时辰便去用饭，大厅里配膳门口敲一声响锣，全家人便从各自的窝里出来，穿着那些古怪衣裳、板着那些正经面孔，就像咱们乡下长廊里挂的那些画像上画的。
汉弗莱
嗬，你这野小子！
汤姆
那时候还得等一个穿黑袍的道学先生对着饭菜念叨几句，才能动刀叉，倒好像厨子做得还不够熟似的。

===Original===
Humphry
Sirrah, who do you prate after? Despising men of sacred characters! I hope you never heard my good young master talk so like a profligate.
Tom
Sir, I say you put upon me, when I first came to town, about being orderly, and the doctrine of wearing shams to make linen last clean a fortnight, keeping my clothes fresh, and wearing a frock within doors.
Humphry
Sirrah, I gave you those lessons because I supposed at that time your master and you might have dined at home every day, and cost you nothing; then you might have made a good family servant. But the gang you have frequented since at chocolate houses and taverns, in a continual round of noise and extravagance﻿—
Tom
I don’t know what you heavy inmates call noise and extravagance; but we gentlemen, who are well fed, and cut a figure, sir, think it a fine life, and that we must be very pretty fellows who are kept only to be looked at.
Humphry
Very well, sir, I hope the fashion of being lewd and extravagant, despising of decency and order, is almost at an end, since it has arrived at persons of your quality.
Tom
Master Humphry, ha! ha! you were an unhappy lad to be sent up to town in such queer days as you were. Why, now, sir, the lackeys are the men of pleasure of the age, the top gamesters; and many a laced coat about town have had their education in our parti-coloured regiment. We are false lovers; have a taste of music, poetry, billet-doux, dress, politics; ruin damsels; and when we are tired of this lewd town, and have a mind to take up, whip into our masters’ wigs and linen, and marry fortunes.

===Chinese===
汉弗莱
小子，你这是学了谁的腔调？竟敢讥诮圣职之人！我但愿你从没听我那善良的小主人说过这等放荡话。
汤姆
老爷，我说的是：我刚进城那会儿，您拿规矩压我，还教训我那套道理——戴假领子，好叫衬衣经得起半个月干净；爱惜衣裳；屋里穿罩衫。
汉弗莱
小子，我那些教训，是因为我那时想着，你主人同你兴许天天在家用饭，一个子儿不用花；那样你倒能做个像样的当家仆人。可你后来混的那伙人——巧克力铺里、酒馆里，成天吵吵嚷嚷、挥霍无度——
汤姆
我不知道你们这些闷葫芦管什么叫吵嚷、什么叫挥霍；可我们这些吃得好、有派头的老爷们，倒觉得这才叫好日子；养着就为给人看的，想必要算是很体面的人物了。
汉弗莱
好得很，先生，我看这放浪挥霍、目无体统的时髦，快要到头了——它都已经传到您这等人物身上了。
汤姆
汉弗莱老爷，哈！哈！您真是个苦命人，赶在那么古板的年月进城里来。嗐，如今呀，老爷，跟班才是这时代的风流人物、头等的赌客；城里多少位穿花边外套的爷，都是从我们这支花衣军团里受的教育。我们做虚情假意的情人；懂点音乐、诗歌、情书、衣饰、政治；糟蹋姑娘家；等把这淫靡的城玩腻了、想安定下来，便转眼套上主人的假发和细麻布衬衣，娶下一份家私。

===Original===
Humphry
Hey-day!
Tom
Nay, sir, our order is carried up to the highest dignities and distinctions; step but into the Painted Chamber,17 and by our titles you’d take us all for men of quality. Then, again, come down to the Court of Requests, and you see us all laying our broken heads together for the good of the nation; and though we never carry a question nemine contradicente, yet this I can say, with a safe conscience (and I wish every gentleman of our cloth could lay his hand upon his heart and say the same), that I never took so much as a single mug of beer for my vote in all my life.
Humphry
Sirrah, there is no enduring your extravagance; I’ll hear you prate no longer. I wanted to see you to enquire how things go with your master, as far as you understand them; I suppose he knows he is to be married today.
Tom
Ay, sir, he knows it, and is dressed as gay as the sun; but, between you and I, my dear, he has a very heavy heart under all that gaiety. As soon as he was dressed I retired, but overheard him sigh in the most heavy manner. He walked thoughtfully to and fro in the room, then went into his closet; when he came out he gave me this for his mistress, whose maid, you know﻿—
Humphry
Is passionately fond of your fine person.
Tom
The poor fool is so tender, and loves to hear me talk of the world, and the plays, operas, and ridottos18 for the winter, the parks and Belsize19 for our summer diversions; and “Lard!” says she, “you are so wild, but you have a world of humour.”

===Chinese===
汉弗莱
嗬，这是什么世道！
汤姆
还不止呢，老爷，我们这一行已经升到顶高的尊严和品级里去了；您只消迈进彩绘厅（Painted Chamber）17 一步，听我们的头衔，准把我们个个当成上流人物。再往下走到请愿厅（Court of Requests），您又看见我们一个个凑着打破的脑袋，为国家的利益共商大计；虽说我们从不曾无一人反对地通过什么议案，可这句话我敢凭良心说（但愿我们这一行的每位爷们都能把手放在心口说同样的话）：我这一辈子，从没为自己那一票收过哪怕一大杯啤酒。
汉弗莱
小子，你这份荒唐真叫人受不了；我不再听你胡扯了。我找你来，是想打听打听你主人的事，就你所知道的办到了哪一步；我想他知道今儿就要成婚了吧。
汤姆
是，老爷，他知道，衣裳也穿得像太阳一样光鲜；可是咱们私下说，亲爱的，那一身光鲜底下，他揣着一颗沉重的心。他一穿戴齐整我便退了出来，却听见他重重地叹气。他在屋里来回踱步，若有所思，随后进了小书房；出来的时候，把这个交给我叫他送到他小姐那儿，她那位贴身丫头，你知道——
汉弗莱
正痴痴地迷着你这份好模样呢。
汤姆
那可怜的傻人儿心肠那样软，最爱听我讲世面：冬天的戏文、歌剧、里多托舞会，18 夏天寻乐去的公园和贝尔塞斯（Belsize）；19 她还说："天哪！你可真野，可你这一肚子风趣。"

===Original===
Humphry
Coxcomb! Well, but why don’t you run with your master’s letter to Mrs. Lucinda, as he ordered you?
Tom
Because Mrs. Lucinda is not so easily come at as you think for.
Humphry
Not easily come at? Why, sirrah, are not her father and my old master agreed that she and Mr. Bevil are to be one flesh before tomorrow morning?
Tom
It’s no matter for that; her mother, it seems, Mrs. Sealand, has not agreed to it; and you must know, Mr. Humphry, that in that family the grey mare is the better horse.
Humphry
What dost thou mean?
Tom
In one word, Mrs. Sealand pretends to have a will of her own, and has provided a relation of hers, a stiff, starched philosopher, and a wise fool, for her daughter; for which reason, for these ten days past, she has suffered no message nor letter from my master to come near her.

===Chinese===
汉弗莱
花花公子！好了，你怎么不照你主人的吩咐，把信给露辛达小姐送去？
汤姆
因为露辛达小姐可不像您想的那么容易见得着。
汉弗莱
不容易见得着？嗐，小子，她父亲同我老主人不是都讲定了，赶明儿天亮之前，她同贝维尔先生就要合为一体了么？
汤姆
那可不顶事；她母亲，就是西兰夫人，看样子还没点头呢；而且您得知道，汉弗莱老爷，那一户人家，是牝鸡司晨的。
汉弗莱
你这话怎么讲？
汤姆
一句话，西兰夫人自称自有主张，早给她女儿备下了一位自家亲戚——一个硬邦邦、浆得笔挺的哲学家，一位聪明的傻子；为着这个缘故，这十天来，我主人捎的口信也好、书信也好，一概不许近她的身。

===Original===
Humphry
And where had you this intelligence?
Tom
From a foolish fond soul that can keep nothing from me; one that will deliver this letter too, if she is rightly managed.
Humphry
What! her pretty handmaid, Mrs. Phillis?
Tom
Even she, sir; this is the very hour, you know, she usually comes hither, under a pretence of a visit to your housekeeper, forsooth, but in reality to have a glance at﻿—
Humphry
Your sweet face, I warrant you.
Tom
Nothing else in nature; you must know, I love to fret and play with the little wanton.

===Chinese===
汉弗莱
这消息你又是从哪儿得来的？
汤姆
从一个又痴心又没心眼的人儿那儿，她什么事都瞒不住我；只要调停得法，这封信她也肯代送。
汉弗莱
什么！她那位俏丽的贴身丫头，菲利斯姑娘（Mrs. Phillis）？
汤姆
正是她，老爷；您知道，往常正是这个时辰她上这儿来，说是拜访贵府的管家娘子，其实呢，是要瞅一眼——
汉弗莱
瞅一眼你那张俏脸蛋，我敢说。
汤姆
天底下再没别的了；您得知道，我就爱逗弄那小浪蹄子，跟她耍耍。

===Original===
Humphry
Play with the little wanton! What will this world come to!
Tom
I met her this morning in a new manteau and petticoat, not a bit the worse for her lady’s wearing; and she has always new thoughts and new airs with new clothes﻿—then she never fails to steal some glance or gesture from every visitant at their house; and is, indeed, the whole town of coquets at secondhand. But here she comes; in one motion she speaks and describes herself better than all the words in the world can.
Humphry
Then I hope, dear sir, when your own affair is over, you will be so good as to mind your master’s with her.
Tom
Dear Humphry, you know my master is my friend, and those are people I never forget.
Humphry
Sauciness itself! but I’ll leave you to do your best for him.
*Exit.*
*Enter **Phillis**.20*

===Chinese===
汉弗莱
跟那小浪蹄子耍耍！这世道要变成什么样子哟！
汤姆
今儿早上我碰见她，穿了件新斗篷裙，她小姐穿过的，一点没走样；她呀，新衣裳一上身，必是新鲜念头、新鲜做派——还有，府里来的每位客人，她总要不露声色地偷学一瞥眼风、一个身段；说真的，全城的卖俏娘们儿，都叫她在二手货里凑齐了。可她来了；她一个举动，比世上一切言语都更能替她自己说话、描画她自己。
汉弗莱
那么我指望，亲爱的先生，您自己的事一了，行行好也上心替您主人跟她把事办了。
汤姆
亲爱的汉弗莱，您知道我主人是我的朋友，这样的人我永远不会忘记。
汉弗莱
好一张放肆的嘴！我去吧，你替他尽力去办。
*下。*
*菲利斯（Phillis）上。20*

===Original===
Phillis
Oh, Mr. Thomas, is Mrs. Sugar-key at home? Lard, one is almost ashamed to pass along the streets! The town is quite empty, and nobody of fashion left in it; and the ordinary people do so stare to see anything, dressed like a woman of condition, as it were on the same floor with them, pass by. Alas! alas! it is a sad thing to walk. O fortune! fortune!
Tom
What! a sad thing to walk? Why, Madam Phillis, do you wish yourself lame?
Phillis
No, Mr. Tom, but I wish I were generally carried in a coach or chair, and of a fortune neither to stand nor go, but to totter, or slide, to be shortsighted, or stare, to fleer in the face, to look distant, to observe, to overlook, yet all become me; and, if I was rich, I could twire21 and loll as well as the best of them. Oh, Tom! Tom! is it not a pity that you should be so great a coxcomb, and I so great a coquet, and yet be such poor devils as we are?
Tom
Mrs. Phillis, I am your humble servant for that﻿—
Phillis
Yes, Mr. Thomas, I know how much you are my humble servant, and know what you said to Mrs. Judy, upon seeing her in one of her lady’s cast manteaus: That anyone would have thought her the lady, and that she had ordered the other to wear it till it sat easy; for now only it was becoming. To my lady it was only a covering, to Mrs. Judy it was a habit. This you said, after somebody or other. Oh, Tom! Tom! thou art as false and as base as the best gentleman of them all; but, you wretch, talk to me no more on the old odious subject﻿—don’t, I say.

===Chinese===
菲利斯
噢，托马斯少爷，糖钥匙太太（Mrs. Sugar-key）在家吗？天哪，如今在街上走路简直叫人害臊！城里空荡荡的，有身份的人一个不剩；平头百姓见了个打扮得像贵妇的人打身边过——仿佛同他们站在一块地板上似的——眼睛都瞪直了。唉！唉！走路真是桩苦事。哦命运！命运！
汤姆
什么！走路是桩苦事？嗐，菲利斯女士，您莫非巴望自己瘸了腿？
菲利斯
不是，汤姆少爷，可我巴望出门一律有马车或轿子抬着，托生一份既不必站着、也不必走路的财运——只管摇摇曳曳、溜溜滑滑，眼睛近视也成、直瞪也成，照面撇嘴也成、端架子不理人也成，顾盼也成、睥睨也成，样样都与我相称；我要是有了钱，斜眼瞟人、21 懒洋洋地倚着，保险不输给她们里头最讲究的。噢，汤姆！汤姆！你做了这么大的花花公子，我做了这么大的俏丽娘们，到头来却还是我们这对穷鬼，你说可惜不可惜？
汤姆
菲利斯女士，这一点上我可是您的恭顺仆人——
菲利斯
是啊，托马斯少爷，我当然知道您多么是我恭顺的仆人，也知道您见了朱迪姑娘（Mrs. Judy）穿她小姐一件旧斗篷时说的话：说谁都要当她才是那位小姐，还说她吩咐另一位先穿着，等穿得伏帖了才算称身——到如今才穿得出样子。对小姐那不过是件遮身的东西，对朱迪姑娘才是一件行头。这话是您跟着某某人的腔调说的。噢，汤姆！汤姆！你同他们里头最体面的爷们一样虚情、一样下作；可是你这坏东西，那桩老掉牙的腌臜题目别再跟我提了——不许提，我说。

===Original===
Tom
I know not how to resist your commands, madam. *In a submissive tone, retiring.*
Phillis
Commands about parting are grown mighty easy to you of late.
Tom
Oh, I have her; I have nettled and put her into the right temper to be wrought upon and set a-prating. *Aside.*﻿—Why, truly, to be plain with you, Mrs. Phillis, I can take little comfort of late in frequenting your house.
Phillis
Pray, Mr. Thomas, what is it all of a sudden offends your nicety at our house?
Tom
I don’t care to speak particulars, but I dislike the whole.

===Chinese===
汤姆
小姐的吩咐，小人不知如何违抗才好。*以恭顺的口气，退后。*
菲利斯
说到分手的吩咐，你近来倒是应得越来越顺溜了。
汤姆
噢，到手了；我撩着了她，正好拿捏她这股脾气，好生揉搓，叫她打开话匣子。*旁白。*——嗐，说真的，对您直言吧，菲利斯女士，近来往贵府跑，我实在没多少乐趣。
菲利斯
请问，托马斯少爷，是什么事忽然冲撞了您那娇贵的讲究？
汤姆
细节我懒得说，反正整个儿我都看不入眼。

===Original===
Phillis
I thank you, sir, I am a part of that whole.
Tom
Mistake me not, good Phillis.
Phillis
Good Phillis! Saucy enough. But however﻿—
Tom
I say, it is that thou art a part, which gives me pain for the disposition of the whole. You must know, madam, to be serious, I am a man, at the bottom, of prodigious nice honour. You are too much exposed to company at your house. To be plain, I don’t like so many, that would be your mistress’s lovers, whispering to you.
Phillis
Don’t think to put that upon me. You say this, because I wrung you to the heart when I touched your guilty conscience about Judy.

===Chinese===
菲利斯
多谢您了，少爷，那个"整个儿"里就有我一份。
汤姆
别错怪我，好菲利斯。
菲利斯
"好菲利斯"！嘴上倒甜。不过——
汤姆
我说的是：正因为你身在其中，那整个儿的情形才叫我痛心。您得知道，小姐，说正经的，我骨子里是个极其讲究名誉的人。在贵府上，你抛头露面得太厉害了。直说了吧，那么多想当你小姐情人的家伙凑在你耳边嘀咕，我不喜欢。
菲利斯
别想把这套栽到我头上。你这么说，是因为我点到朱迪那桩事、戳着了你的良心，叫你痛到心口了。

===Original===
Tom
Ah, Phillis! Phillis! if you but knew my heart!
Phillis
I know too much on’t.
Tom
Nay, then, poor Crispo’s22 fate and mine are one. Therefore give me leave to say, or sing at least, as he does upon the same occasion﻿—“*Se vedette*,” etc. *Sings.*
Phillis
What, do you think I’m to be fobbed off with a song? I don’t question but you have sung the same to Mrs. Judy too.

===Chinese===
汤姆
唉，菲利斯！菲利斯！你要是知道我的心就好了！
菲利斯
我知道得太多了。
汤姆
唉，这么说，可怜的克里斯波（Crispo）22 的命运同我的就是一样了。所以请容我学他在这同一场合的样子，说一句，或者至少唱一句——"*Se vedette*"，等等。*唱。*
菲利斯
什么，你当一支歌就能把我打发掉？我不怀疑这支歌你也给朱迪姑娘唱过。

===Original===
Tom
Don’t disparage your charms, good Phillis, with jealousy of so worthless an object; besides, she is a poor hussy, and if you doubt the sincerity of my love, you will allow me true to my interest. You are a fortune, Phillis.
Phillis
What would the fop be at now? In good time, indeed, you shall be setting up for a fortune!
Tom
Dear Mrs. Phillis, you have such a spirit that we shall never be dull in marriage when we come together. But I tell you, you are a fortune, and you have an estate in my hands. *He pulls out a purse, she eyes it.*
Phillis
What pretence have I to what is in your hands, Mr. Tom?
Tom
As thus: there are hours, you know, when a lady is neither pleased or displeased; neither sick or well; when she lolls or loiters; when she’s without desires﻿—from having more of everything than she knows what to do with.

===Chinese===
汤姆
好菲利斯，别为那么个不值一提的东西吃醋，反倒屈了您的魅力；再说，她不过是个可怜的傻丫头，倘若您信不过我爱情的真诚，总还承认我对自己的利害是真心实意的。菲利斯，你是一份财产啊。
菲利斯
这花花公子如今又要干什么？好啊，真是的，您倒做起"一份财产"的主意来了！
汤姆
亲爱的菲利斯女士，您有这么一副心性，咱们将来凑成一家，日子绝不会闷。可我告诉你，你就是一份财产，而且你在我手里就有一宗产业。*他掏出一只钱袋，她盯着看。*
菲利斯
汤姆少爷，您手里那东西，我凭什么名分来沾？
汤姆
这么说吧：您知道，小姐们有几个时辰，既不欢喜也不恼；既不病也不健；懒懒地倚着，闲闲地荡着；什么念想也没有——因为样样都多得叫她不知怎么打发。

===Original===
Phillis
Well, what then?
Tom
When she has not life enough to keep her bright eyes quite open, to look at her own dear image in the glass.
Phillis
Explain thyself, and don’t be so fond of thy own prating.
Tom
There are also prosperous and good-natured moments: as when a knot or a patch is happily fixed; when the complexion particularly flourishes.
Phillis
Well, what then? I have not patience!
Tom
Why, then﻿—or on the like occasions﻿—we servants who have skill to know how to time business, see when such a pretty folded thing as this *shows a letter* may be presented, laid, or dropped, as best suits the present humour. And, madam, because it is a long wearisome journey to run through all the several stages of a lady’s temper, my master, who is the most reasonable man in the world, presents you this to bear your charges on the road. *Gives her the purse.*

===Chinese===
菲利斯
好，往下呢？
汤姆
当她连把那双明眸睁全的气力都没有，顾不上照镜子里自己那可爱影子的时辰。
菲利斯
把话说明白，别只顾卖弄你那套絮叨。
汤姆
也有顺遂和脾气温和的时刻：比如蝴蝶结或美人斑恰巧贴得妥帖；比如脸色格外红润光鲜。
菲利斯
好，再往下呢？我可没耐性了！
汤姆
嗐，便在那样的时辰——或者诸如此类的场合——我们这些懂得瞅时机办事的仆人，就瞧得出这样一件折得漂漂亮亮的小东西*出示一封信*该在什么时候递上、搁下或失落，才最合眼下的心意。而且，小姐，因为逐站走完一位小姐脾性的全程，是段又长又累的旅程，我家主人——世上最通情达理的人——送您这个，好充您一路的盘缠。*把钱袋给她。*

===Original===
Phillis
Now you think me a corrupt hussy.
Tom
O fie, I only think you’ll take the letter.
Phillis
Nay, I know you do, but I know my own innocence; I take it for my mistress’s sake.
Tom
I know it, my pretty one, I know it.
Phillis
Yes, I say I do it, because I would not have my mistress deluded by one who gives no proof of his passion; but I’ll talk more of tips as you see me on my way home. No, Tom, I assure thee, I take this trash of thy master’s, not for the value of the thing, but as it convinces me he has a true respect for my mistress. I remember a verse to the purpose﻿—
They may be false who languish and complain,
But they who part with money never feign.
*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
菲利斯
这会儿您当我是个见钱眼开的贱蹄子了。
汤姆
呸，瞧您说的，我不过想着您肯把信收下。
菲利斯
得了，我知道您当我是，可我知道自己清白；我收它是为了我们小姐。
汤姆
我知道，我的小人儿，我知道。
菲利斯
是，我说我收，是因为我不愿我们小姐叫一个拿不出情意凭据的人哄了去；不过赏钱的事，等你送我回家的路上再细说。不，汤姆，我告诉你，你主人这堆黄白物，我收下不看它值多少，只看它叫我相信他对我们小姐是一片真心。我记得有一联诗正说的是这个——
呻吟哀怨的，可能全是虚情，
肯掏钱的人，从来不作假。
*众人下。*

===Original===
Scene II. Bevil Jr.’s Lodgings.
***Bevil Jr.**, reading.*
Bevil Jr.
These moral writers practise virtue after death. This charming vision of Mirza!23 Such an author consulted in a morning sets the spirit for the vicissitudes of the day better than the glass does a man’s person. But what a day have I to go through! to put on an easy look with an aching heart! If this lady my father urges me to marry should not refuse me, my dilemma is insupportable. But why should I fear it? Is not she in equal distress with me? Has not the letter I have sent her this morning confessed my inclination to another? Nay, have I not moral assurances of her engagements, too, to my friend Myrtle? It’s impossible but she must give in to it; for, sure, to be denied is a favour any man may pretend to. It must be so﻿—Well, then, with the assurance of being rejected, I think I may confidently say to my father, I am ready to marry her. Then let me resolve upon, what I am not very good at, though it is an honest dissimulation.
*Enter **Tom**.*
Tom
Sir John Bevil, sir, is in the next room.
Bevil Jr.
Dunce! Why did not you bring him in?

===Chinese===
第二场　小贝维尔寓所。
*小贝维尔（Bevil Jr.）在读信。*
小贝维尔
这些道德作家，人死了德行才见功效。米尔扎（Mirza）的这幅迷人幻景！23 这样的书清晨一读，为人应付这一天的世变沉浮养足心神，胜过镜子为人的仪容做的准备。可我今天要挨过的是怎样一天！心头作痛，还得挂一副轻松的面孔！倘若父亲逼我娶的这位小姐竟不拒绝我，我可就进退两难、无地自容了。可我何必怕这个？她不是同我一样为难么？我今天早晨送去的那封信，不是已经招认了我的心另有所属么？再说，她同我朋友默特尔（Myrtle）的婚约，这层我不是也有充分的德行上的把据么？她不可能不点头成全；因为被拒，原是任何人都可以指望的一件恩惠。必然如此——那么，既然笃定要吃闭门羹，我想我不妨放心对父亲说：我随时可以娶她。接下来就得打定主意，使出我并不擅长的本领——一种诚实的佯装。
*汤姆上。*
汤姆
先生，约翰·贝维尔爵士在隔壁屋里。
小贝维尔
蠢材！怎么不请他进来？

===Original===
Tom
I told him, sir, you were in your closet.
Bevil Jr.
I thought you had known, sir, it was my duty to see my father anywhere. *Going himself to the door.*
Tom
The devil’s in my master! he has always more wit than I have. *Aside.*
***Bevil Jr.**, introducing **Sir John**.*
Bevil Jr.
Sir, you are the most gallant, the most complaisant of all parents. Sure, ’tis not a compliment to say these lodgings are yours. Why would you not walk in, sir?
John Bevil
I was loth to interrupt you unseasonably on your wedding-day.

===Chinese===
汤姆
我禀报他了，先生，说您在小书房里。
小贝维尔
我还当你懂事呢，先生，迎候父亲，无论何处都是我的本分。*亲自走向门口。*
汤姆
我主人真是邪了门了！他永远比我多一个心眼。*旁白。*
*小贝维尔引约翰爵士入。*
小贝维尔
父亲，您是天下做父母的里头最豪爽、最殷勤的一位。说这寓所本是您的，想必不算奉承吧。您怎么不肯径直进来呢，父亲？
约翰·贝维尔
你大喜的日子，我不愿不识时务地打搅你。

===Original===
Bevil Jr.
One to whom I am beholden for my birthday might have used less ceremony.
John Bevil
Well, son, I have intelligence you have writ to your mistress this morning. It would please my curiosity to know the contents of a wedding-day letter; for courtship must then be over.
Bevil Jr.
I assure you, sir, there was no insolence in it upon the prospect of such a vast fortune’s being added to our family; but much acknowledgment of the lady’s greater desert.
John Bevil
But, dear Jack, are you in earnest in all this? And will you really marry her?
Bevil Jr.
Did I ever disobey any command of yours, sir? nay, any inclination that I saw you bent upon?

===Chinese===
小贝维尔
我这条命还是仰赖您才有的，本可以少讲些客套。
约翰·贝维尔
好了，儿子，我得到消息，你今天早上给你的小姐写了信。我很想满足一下好奇心，看看这婚日书信里写了什么；因为到了这一天，求爱总该完结了。
小贝维尔
您放心，父亲，信里没有一点仗着那样一份大家业将归入我们家门而生的骄矜；倒是对小姐更优越的人品多所敬服。
约翰·贝维尔
可是，亲爱的杰克，这一切你是当真的么？你真的要娶她？
小贝维尔
父亲，我可曾违逆过您哪一道命令？甚至，您属意的哪一桩心愿？

===Original===
John Bevil
Why, I can’t say you have, son; but methinks in this whole business, you have not been so warm as I could have wished you. You have visited her, it’s true, but you have not been particular. Everyone knows you can say and do as handsome things as any man; but you have done nothing but lived in the general﻿—been complaisant only.
Bevil Jr.
As I am ever prepared to marry if you bid me, so I am ready to let it alone if you will have me.
***Humphry** enters, unobserved.*
John Bevil
Look you there now! why, what am I to think of this so absolute and so indifferent a resignation?
Bevil Jr.
Think? that I am still your son, sir. Sir, you have been married, and I have not. And you have, sir, found the inconvenience there is when a man weds with too much love in his head. I have been told, sir, that at the time you married, you made a mighty bustle on the occasion. There was challenging and fighting, scaling walls, locking up the lady, and the gallant under an arrest for fear of killing all his rivals. Now, sir, I suppose you having found the ill consequences of these strong passions and prejudices, in preference of one woman to another, in case of a man’s becoming a widower﻿—
John Bevil
How is this?
Bevil Jr.

===Chinese===
约翰·贝维尔
嗐，这倒说不上，儿子；可是在这整桩事情里，依我看你一直不够火热，不如我所愿。你拜访过她，这不假，可你从没有特别献过殷勤。谁都知道，漂亮话、漂亮事你做得不输任何人；可你事事都只泛泛而过——仅止于客气罢了。
小贝维尔
您吩咐我成婚，我随时准备从命；您要我作罢，我也随时准备罢手。
*汉弗莱上，无人察觉。*
约翰·贝维尔
瞧瞧这算什么！嗐，这样绝对、这样无动于衷的听天由命，叫我怎么想？
小贝维尔
怎么想？想我仍是您的儿子，父亲。父亲，您结过婚，我没有。您也已经领教过，一个人脑子里情爱太满时成婚，会有什么不便。听说您当年成婚的时候，为这事闹出好大的动静。下战书的、决斗的、翻墙的、把小姐锁起来的，还有那位情郎遭了拘押——生怕把所有情敌都杀死。如今，父亲，我想您是尝过了这种种强烈情热与偏见的恶果——把一个女人置于另一个女人之上，万一男人做了鳏夫——
约翰·贝维尔
这话从何说起？
小贝维尔

===Original===
I say, sir, experience has made you wiser in your care of me; for, sir, since you lost my dear mother, your time has been so heavy, so lonely, and so tasteless, that you are so good as to guard me against the like unhappiness, by marrying me prudentially by way of bargain and sale. For, as you well judge, a woman that is espoused for a fortune, is yet a better bargain if she dies; for then a man still enjoys what he did marry, the money, and is disencumbered of what he did not marry, the woman.
John Bevil
But pray, sir, do you think Lucinda, then, a woman of such little merit?
Bevil Jr.
Pardon me, sir, I don’t carry it so far neither; I am rather afraid I shall like her too well; she has, for one of her fortune, a great many needless and superfluous good qualities.
John Bevil
I am afraid, son, there’s something I don’t see yet, something that’s smothered under all this raillery.
Bevil Jr.

===Chinese===
我是说，父亲，是经验叫您在管教我这件事上变得更有智慧；因为，父亲，自从您失去了我亲爱的母亲，您的日子过得那样沉重、那样孤单、那样没滋没味，于是您好心提防我遭同样的不幸，把我精打细算地、照着一手交钱一手交货的买卖路数嫁娶。因为正如您的高见：为财产娶来的女人，若是死了，更是一桩好买卖；因为那时节，男人仍旧坐享他当初娶的东西——钱财——又卸脱了他不曾娶的东西——女人。
约翰·贝维尔
那么请问，先生，你是把露辛达看成这样无德无貌的女人了？
小贝维尔
恕我不敢苟同，父亲，我也没说到那一步；我倒是怕自己会太喜欢她；以她那样的家财，她竟有那么多多余的、不必要的良好品质。
约翰·贝维尔
我怕，儿子，这里面还有我没看透的东西，有什么东西捂在这一片插科打诨底下。
小贝维尔

===Original===
Not in the least, sir. If the lady is dressed and ready, you see I am. I suppose the lawyers are ready too.
Humphry
This may grow warm if I don’t interpose. *Aside.*﻿—Sir, Mr. Sealand is at the coffeehouse, and has sent to speak with you.
John Bevil
Oh! that’s well! Then I warrant the lawyers are ready. Son, you’ll be in the way, you say.
Bevil Jr.
If you please, sir, I’ll take a chair, and go to Mr. Sealand’s, where the young lady and I will wait your leisure.
John Bevil
By no means. The old fellow will be so vain if he sees﻿—
Bevil Jr.
Ay; but the young lady, sir, will think me so indifferent.

===Chinese===
一点儿没有，父亲。小姐若已妆扮停当，您看我也就绪了。我料想律师们也都齐备了。
汉弗莱
我再不插进去，这就要热闹了。*旁白。*——老爷，西兰先生在咖啡馆里，遣人来请您过去说话。
约翰·贝维尔
噢！那好！那律师们想必也就绪了。儿子，你说你随时听候。
小贝维尔
父亲若允许，我就叫一顶轿子，去西兰先生府上，同那位小姐在那儿恭候您得便。
约翰·贝维尔
万万不可。那老家伙见着就要得意——
小贝维尔
是啊；可是父亲，那位小姐却要想我太冷淡了。

===Original===
Humphry
Ay, there you are right; press your readiness to go to the bride﻿—he won’t let you. *Aside to **Bevil Jr.***
Bevil Jr.
Are you sure of that? *Aside to **Humphry**.*
Humphry
How he likes being prevented. *Aside.*
John Bevil
No, no. You are an hour or two too early. *Looking on his watch.*
Bevil Jr.
You’ll allow me, sir, to think it too late to visit a beautiful, virtuous young woman, in the pride and bloom of life, ready to give herself to my arms; and to place her happiness or misery, for the future, in being agreeable or displeasing to me, is a﻿—Call a chair.

===Chinese===
汉弗莱
对，这话在理；只管催着要去新娘那儿——他不会放的。*向小贝维尔旁白。*
小贝维尔
您拿得稳？*向汉弗莱旁白。*
汉弗莱
瞧他多受用别人替他拦着。*旁白。*
约翰·贝维尔
不，不。你早到了一两个钟头。*看表。*
小贝维尔
父亲也容我想来：去拜见一位正值韶华、如花盛放、甘愿投入我怀抱的美丽贤淑的女子，只怕已经嫌迟了；她今后的幸福或不幸，全系于讨我喜欢还是惹我不快，这可真是——叫顶轿子来。

===Original===
John Bevil
No, no, no, dear Jack; this Sealand is a moody old fellow. There’s no dealing with some people but by managing with indifference. We must leave to him the conduct of this day. It is the last of his commanding his daughter.
Bevil Jr.
Sir, he can’t take it ill, that I am impatient to be hers.
John Bevil
Pray let me govern in this matter; you can’t tell how humorsome old fellows are. There’s no offering reason to some of ’em, especially when they are rich.﻿—If my son should see him before I’ve brought old Sealand into better temper, the match would be impracticable. *Aside.*
Humphry
Pray, sir, let me beg you to let Mr. Bevil go.﻿—See whether he will or not. *Aside to **Sir John**.*﻿—*Then to **Bevil Jr.*** Pray, sir, command yourself; since you see my master is positive, it is better you should not go.
Bevil Jr.
My father commands me, as to the object of my affections; but I hope he will not, as to the warmth and height of them.
John Bevil
So! I must even leave things as I found them; and in the meantime, at least, keep old Sealand out of his sight﻿—Well, son, I’ll go myself and take orders in your affair. You’ll be in the way, I suppose, if I send to you. I’ll leave your old friend with you﻿—Humphry, don’t let him stir, d’ye hear?﻿—Your servant, your servant.

===Chinese===
约翰·贝维尔
不，不，不，亲爱的杰克；这西兰是个喜怒无常的老家伙。同有些人打交道，只有摆出冷淡一路才成。这一天便交给他来主持吧。这也是他号令他女儿的最后一天了。
小贝维尔
父亲，我巴望早些归属她，他总不致见怪吧。
约翰·贝维尔
这件事求你让我做主；你不知道老家伙们的脾气有多古怪。同他们中有些人没法讲道理，尤其是他们有钱的时候。——倘若我儿子在我把老西兰哄得脾气转好之前撞见他，这门亲事就要砸了。*旁白。*
汉弗莱
求您了，老爷，容我劝您放贝维尔先生去吧。——您瞧他放不放。*向约翰爵士旁白。*——*转向小贝维尔。*求您了，先生，且克制着；您既见我家老爷主意已定，还是不去为好。
小贝维尔
我情感之所归，父亲可以命令我；但情感的温度与高度，我想他总不至于也下命令。
约翰·贝维尔
好吧！我只好暂且听其自然；至少眼下，别让老西兰撞见他——那么，儿子，我亲自去，替你的事领受指示。我若遣人叫你，你随时听候。我把你的老朋友留给你——汉弗莱，别让他走动，听见没有？——失陪了，失陪了。

===Original===
*Exit **Sir John**.*
Humphry
I have a sad time on’t, sir, between you and my master. I see you are unwilling, and I know his violent inclinations for the match.﻿—I must betray neither, and yet deceive you both, for your common good. Heaven grant a good end of this matter.﻿—But there is a lady, sir, that gives your father much trouble and sorrow.﻿—You’ll pardon me.
Bevil Jr.
Humphry, I know thou art a friend to both, and in that confidence I dare tell thee, that lady is a woman of honour and virtue. You may assure yourself I never will marry without my father’s consent. But give me leave to say, too, this declaration does not come up to a promise that I will take whomsoever he pleases.
Humphry
Come, sir, I wholly understand you. You would engage my services to free you from this woman whom my master intends you, to make way, in time, for the woman you have really a mind to.
Bevil Jr.
Honest Humphry, you have always been a useful friend to my father and myself; I beg you continue your good offices, and don’t let us come to the necessity of a dispute; for, if we should dispute, I must either part with more than life, or lose the best of fathers.
Humphry
My dear master, were I but worthy to know this secret, that so near concerns you, my life, my all should be engaged to serve you. This, sir, I dare promise, that I am sure I will and can be secret: your trust, at worst, but leaves you where you were; and if I cannot serve you, I will at once be plain and tell you so.

===Chinese===
*约翰爵士下。*
汉弗莱
夹在您同我家老爷中间，我这日子真难熬，先生。我看得出您不情愿，也知道他为这门亲事一片火热。——我谁也不能辜负，却得把你们两位都瞒着，全为你们两家的好处。上天保佑这事有个好结局。——不过，先生，有一位小姐，叫您父亲操了许多心、添了许多愁。——您要恕我直言。
小贝维尔
汉弗莱，我知道你是我们俩共同的朋友，正是凭着这份信任，我才敢告诉你：那位小姐是位有名节、有德行的女子。你只管放心，不经我父亲应允，我绝不成婚。可也容我说一句：这句声明，并不等于许下诺言，说他中意谁我便娶谁。
汉弗莱
得了，先生，我全明白您的意思了。您是要借重我这点微劳，替您从我家老爷属意的这位女子身边脱身出来，好迟早给您真正属意的那位让出路来。
小贝维尔
忠厚的汉弗莱，你一向是我父亲和我的患难益友；求你继续斡旋，别弄到我们爷俩非争执不可的地步；因为一旦争执，我要么就得舍掉比性命还贵重的东西，要么就得失去天下最好的父亲。
汉弗莱
我亲爱的主人，我若有福分知晓这桩与您攸关的秘密，我这条命、我的一切都该用来侍奉您。这一层，先生，我敢应承：我必定守口如瓶，也守得住：您这一信托，往坏处说，也不过同原处一样；倘若我帮不上忙，我也立刻直说，绝不含糊。

===Original===
Bevil Jr.
That’s all I ask. Thou hast made it now my interest to trust thee. Be patient, then, and hear the story of my heart.
Humphry
I am all attention, sir.
Bevil Jr.
You may remember, Humphry, that in my last travels my father grew uneasy at my making so long a stay at Toulon.
Humphry
I remember it; he was apprehensive some woman had laid hold of you.
Bevil Jr.
His fears were just; for there I first saw this lady. She is of English birth: her father’s name was Danvers﻿—a younger brother of an ancient family, and originally an eminent merchant of Bristol, who, upon repeated misfortunes, was reduced to go privately to the Indies. In this retreat, Providence again grew favourable to his industry, and, in six years’ time, restored him to his former fortunes. On this he sent directions over that his wife and little family should follow him to the Indies. His wife, impatient to obey such welcome orders, would not wait the leisure of a convoy, but took the first occasion of a single ship, and, with her husband’s sister only, and this daughter, then scarce seven years old, undertook the fatal voyage﻿—for here, poor creature, she lost her liberty and life. She and her family, with all they had, were, unfortunately, taken by a privateer from Toulon. Being thus made a prisoner, though as such not ill-treated, yet the fright, the shock, and cruel disappointment, seized with such violence upon her unhealthy frame, she sickened, pined, and died at sea.
Humphry
Poor soul! O the helpless infant!

===Chinese===
小贝维尔
我求的仅此而已。这一来，信任你便成了我的利益所在。那么请耐着性子，听我讲讲这颗心的来历。
汉弗莱
小的洗耳恭听，先生。
小贝维尔
汉弗莱，你或许记得，我上次游历的时候，我在土伦（Toulon）逗留太久，父亲渐渐不安起来。
汉弗莱
我记得；他当时担心有什么女人缠住了您。
小贝维尔
他的忧虑不差；我正是在那儿初见这位小姐。她生为英国人：父亲姓丹弗斯（Danvers）——古老门第的幼子，原是布里斯托尔（Bristol）一位显赫的商人，遭了接二连三的不幸，只得悄然远走印度群岛。在这避难之地，天意又眷顾起他的勤勉，不过六年，便使他重振旧业。于是他传话过来，叫妻子带着年幼的一家去印度群岛团聚。他妻子巴不得遵从这般如意的差遣，等不及护航船队的从容，一有单身船只便搭了上去，带着丈夫的妹妹和这个女儿——那时她还不足七岁——踏上了那致命的航程——因为可怜的人，她就在这航程里失去了自由与性命。她同她一家连同全部财物，不幸被一艘土伦私掠船掳去。她虽身为俘虏，倒不曾受虐待，可是惊骇、打击与残酷的失望，猛然袭上她那孱弱的病体，她一病不起，形销骨立，死在了海上。
汉弗莱
可怜的人！噢，那无依无靠的婴孩！

===Original===
Bevil Jr.
Her sister yet survived, and had the care of her. The captain, too, proved to have humanity, and became a father to her; for having himself married an English woman, and being childless, he brought home into Toulon this her little countrywoman, presenting her, with all her dead mother’s movables of value, to his wife, to be educated as his own adopted daughter.
Humphry
Fortune here seemed again to smile on her.
Bevil Jr.
Only to make her frowns more terrible; for, in his height of fortune, this captain, too, her benefactor, unfortunately was killed at sea; and dying intestate, his estate fell wholly to an advocate, his brother, who, coming soon to take possession, there found (among his other riches) this blooming virgin at his mercy.
Humphry
